# Port spec - fonts, the UI kit, the cursor and the HUD

**Ground truth (do not modify):**

| File | Lines | What it provides |
|---|---|---|
| `E:/SnakeGame/snake/gfx/fonts.py` | 115 | `FontBook` - the named font ladder and its cache |
| `E:/SnakeGame/snake/gfx/ui.py` | 1128 | Panels, text, bars, `Button`, `draw_cursor`, and the whole HUD |

**Companion specs:** `docs/port/render.md` sections 0, 1 and 6 define the shared glow/disc
primitive layer (`stampGlow`, "glow texture cache", "the additive-glow convention"); this
document reuses that vocabulary and does not redefine it. `docs/port/integration.md` §8.1 fixes
the HUD state contract, §9 the READY/GO overlays, §10 the real-dt vs scaled-dt split, and §2.2
the shell-level draw order that owns the cursor. `docs/port/scenes.md` records the *call sites*
of every widget specced here; this document owns the *implementations*. That seam is deliberate.

Coordinate space throughout: **1280x720 design pixels**. Arena rect
`ARENA_X, ARENA_Y, ARENA_W, ARENA_H = 14, 78, 1252, 628`; the HUD strip is `(0, 0, 1280, 78)`.
Angles are radians, `0 = +x`, **y grows downward**, so increasing angle in direct `cos/sin` trig
is **clockwise on screen** - except `pygame.draw.arc`, which is y-up. Both conventions occur
inside `draw_cursor`; see §6.

**Suggested TS homes** (proposed by the sections, and settled here):

| Python | TS home |
|---|---|
| `fonts.py::FontBook` | `web/src/gfx/fonts.ts` - hands out `TextStyleOptions`, never `Text` |
| `_glow_add` / `_blit_glow` / `_rrect_glow` | extend `web/src/gfx/textures.ts`; **no second glow cache** |
| `_panel_surface` / `draw_panel` | `web/src/ui/panel.ts` |
| `_text_pair` / `draw_text` | `web/src/ui/text.ts` |
| `_bar_track` / `_bar_fill` / `draw_bar` | `web/src/ui/bar.ts` |
| `Button` | `web/src/ui/Button.ts` |
| `draw_cursor` | `web/src/ui/Cursor.ts`, owned by the shell (§6.4) |
| `draw_hud` and its widgets | `web/src/ui/hud/` |

## Status of this document

It was written by reading the Python line by line, one agent per slice. **Read this before
trusting a number.**

| Section | Source | Audited |
|---|---|---|
| 0 Fonts | fonts.py, all 115 lines + every call site | no |
| 1 Foundations, caches, local glow | ui.py:1-200 | no |
| 2 Panels / 3 Text / 4 Bars | ui.py:201-354 | no |
| 5 Button | ui.py:355-589 | no |
| 6 Cursor | ui.py:590-662 | no |
| 7 HUD registry, anim, backdrop, odometer | ui.py:663-820 | no |
| 8 HUD widgets | ui.py:821-959 | no |
| 9 `draw_hud` layout | ui.py:960-1128 | written directly from source, re-read in the same pass |

The adversarial audit pass and the cross-section completeness critic that were planned for this
document **did not run** - they were cut off by a spend limit partway through. So:

- Every constant here is a first-pass transcription. Where a number matters, the honest
  verification is to reread that line of `ui.py` while implementing it, and then compare the
  rendered result against `captures/*.png`.
- Two known consequences: nothing has checked the sections against *each other* (a primitive
  may be described twice under different names), and nothing has enumerated `ui.py`'s ~35
  top-level definitions to prove each one is covered.
- The sections do carry their own cross-checks against the TS sources and against
  `captures/*.png`, and several corrected the brief they were given - see the quirk lists.

Verification is perceptual (screenshots vs `E:/SnakeGame/captures/*.png` at 1280x720, which is
exactly the design space), never pixel-diff.

## 0. The font system

**Ground truth:** `E:/SnakeGame/snake/gfx/fonts.py` (115 lines, all of it) plus every call site of
`game.fonts` across `snake/**/*.py`. The Python game must not be modified.

**Suggested TS home:** `web/src/gfx/fonts.ts`, exporting `FontBook` (a class) and the three CSS
family stacks. One instance lives on the shell as `game.fonts`, exactly as `main.py:98` does
(`self.fonts = FontBook()`), which is the name `docs/port/integration.md` §11 already reserves.
Nothing in `web/src/gfx/textures.ts` needs extending: the texture cache is a *pixel* cache keyed on
geometry, the font cache is a `TextStyle` cache keyed on (role, size, bold). They share no keys and
no lifecycle, so this is a new module, not a second glow cache.

**Where the numbers live.** `web/src/data/config.json` is generated by `tools/export_data.py:80` and
carries *only* `snake/config.py` (`"_note": "every tuning constant from snake/config.py"`). Checked:
no key matching `/font/i` exists in `config.json`, `config.ts` or `palette.ts`. The ladder sizes and
the face lists are `gfx/fonts.py` module constants, so they belong as module constants in
`web/src/gfx/fonts.ts` — do **not** hand-edit the generated JSON to hold them. Colours never come
from here: every text colour is a `Theme` field or a `UI_*` constant from `web/src/core/palette.ts`.

### 0.1 What the module is

`FontBook` is a lazy, permanently-cached factory over three *face families* (display / UI / mono),
each resolved once at construction to the first face the host system actually has. It exposes nine
named roles as eager attributes and three on-demand builders. It holds no colour, no alignment and
no drawing — a `pygame.font.Font` is a rasteriser and a metrics oracle, nothing more. Both jobs
survive the port: in Pixi a `TextStyle` is the rasteriser recipe and `CanvasTextMetrics` is the
oracle, so `FontBook` hands out **`TextStyleOptions`, never `Text` objects** (§0.7).

---

### 0.2 The three preference lists

Verbatim from `fonts.py:18-29`, order preserved (best first). Names are pygame-normalised: lowercase,
spaces stripped — that is the form `pygame.font.get_fonts()` returns, which is why they look like
this and why the CSS column below has to re-expand them.

| # | `DISPLAY_FACES` (fonts.py:18-21) | `UI_FACES` (fonts.py:22-25) | `MONO_FACES` (fonts.py:26-29) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `bahnschrift` | `segoeui` | `consolas` |
| 2 | `impact` | `selawik` | `cascadiamono` |
| 3 | `franklingothicheavy` | `helveticaneue` | `menlo` |
| 4 | `arialblack` | `roboto` | `dejavusansmono` |
| 5 | `helveticaneue` | `dejavusans` | `liberationmono` |
| 6 | `dejavusans` | `verdana` | `couriernew` |
| 7 | `verdana` | `arial` | `monospace` |
| 8 | `arial` | — | — |

`_pick(faces)` (fonts.py:32-41) is exactly *first available*:

```python
available = set(pygame.font.get_fonts())   # returns None on any exception
for face in faces:
    if face in available: return face
return None
```

Resolution happens **once**, in `__init__` (fonts.py:56-58) — the three winners are frozen for the
process. A face installed mid-session is never noticed.

#### 0.2.1 How `SysFont` matching differs from a CSS stack

A CSS `font-family` list has the *same* first-available semantics as `_pick`, evaluated per-character
by the browser instead of once by the game. **That equivalence is the port**: the three lists become
three CSS strings, and `_pick` disappears. There is no `document.fonts.check()` loop to write and no
`FontFaceSet` probing — the cascade already does it.

What is *not* equivalent is what pygame does with the name once it has picked it. `pygame.sysfont`
builds a dict `Sysfonts[normalised_name][(bold, italic)] -> path`, and the normaliser **strips style
modifier words** (`light`, `semilight`, `narrow`, `demibold`, …) from the family name before using it
as the key. Consequently sibling families collapse onto one key and the last one enumerated wins the
`(False, False)` slot. Measured on the ground-truth Windows 11 box (pygame 2.6.1, SDL 2.28.4, 301
faces enumerated):

| pygame name | `(False,False)` → | `(True,False)` → | Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bahnschrift` | `C:\Windows\Fonts\bahnschrift.ttf` | *absent* | bold is **synthetic** |
| `segoeui` | `C:\Windows\Fonts\segoeuil.ttf` | `segoeuib.ttf` | non-bold is **Segoe UI Light** |
| `consolas` | `consola.ttf` | `consolab.ttf` | both real |
| `arial` | `C:\Windows\Fonts\ARIALN.TTF` | `ARIALNB.TTF` | resolves to **Arial Narrow** |
| `impact`, `arialblack`, `franklingothicheavy` | `impact.ttf`, `ariblk.ttf`, `FRAHV.TTF` | *absent* | bold synthetic |

Three quirks fall out, all of which the port must copy because the captures in
`E:/SnakeGame/captures/*.png` were rendered with them:

- **`fonts.get(n)` is Segoe UI *Light*, not Regular — ported as a quirk.** `body`, `small`, `tiny`
  and every non-bold `get()` render in weight 300. Visible: `SysFont("segoeui",17).size("SCORE  NO
  ACTIVE POWER-UPS")` = **238** px against Segoe UI Regular's **248** px and Semilight's 243 px — a
  4% width difference on every HUD caption, and a visibly thinner stroke at 14 px. The port must set
  `fontWeight: "300"` on the non-bold UI roles. Using `system-ui` (which is Segoe UI *Regular* on
  Windows) instead of `"Segoe UI"` + weight 300 gets both the wrong weight and the wrong widths.
- **Display bold is fake bold.** `Sysfonts["bahnschrift"]` has no bold entry, so `SysFont(...,
  bold=True)` loads the regular file and calls `set_bold(True)` — pygame's synthetic emboldening.
  Measured at 96 px, `"NEON SERPENT"` is 690 px plain and **699 px** fake-bold (+1.3%), i.e. a very
  light smear. `Bahnschrift` is a *variable* font (`wght` axis), so a browser asked for
  `font-weight: 700` will instantiate a genuinely heavy DIN cut — much heavier than the capture. See
  §0.8 for the recommendation.
- **`arial` in either list means Arial Narrow.** Only reachable if the six faces above it are all
  missing; noted so nobody "fixes" a narrow fallback that is faithful.

CSS expansions of the pygame names (use these, with the modifier words back):

| pygame | CSS family |
|---|---|
| `bahnschrift` | `Bahnschrift` |
| `franklingothicheavy` | `"Franklin Gothic Heavy"` |
| `arialblack` | `"Arial Black"` |
| `helveticaneue` | `"Helvetica Neue"` |
| `segoeui` | `"Segoe UI"` |
| `dejavusans` / `dejavusansmono` | `"DejaVu Sans"` / `"DejaVu Sans Mono"` |
| `cascadiamono` | `"Cascadia Mono"` |
| `liberationmono` | `"Liberation Mono"` |
| `couriernew` | `"Courier New"` |
| `selawik`, `roboto`, `impact`, `verdana`, `arial`, `consolas`, `menlo`, `monospace` | same word, unquoted (`monospace` is the CSS generic and does the same job as pygame's alias) |

---

### 0.3 The named ladder (fonts.py:63-71)

Nine roles, built eagerly in `__init__` so the first frame never pays for them. **`h2` is the odd
one: UI-face bold, not display-face** — the only bold entry in the UI column, and the reason
`display_at(30)` and `h2` are different objects even though both are "30, bold".
`display_at()` (fonts.py:102-104) **always** passes `bold=True`; there is no non-bold display face
anywhere in the game.

| Role | Python (fonts.py) | px | Family | bold | Ground-truth file | `get_height()` | ascent | descent | `get_linesize()` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `huge` | `display_at(96)` :63 | 96 | display | yes (synthetic) | `bahnschrift.ttf` | 96 | 77 | −19 | 116 |
| `title` | `display_at(64)` :64 | 64 | display | yes (synthetic) | `bahnschrift.ttf` | 64 | 51 | −13 | 77 |
| `h1` | `display_at(42)` :65 | 42 | display | yes (synthetic) | `bahnschrift.ttf` | 42 | 34 | −8 | 51 |
| `h2` | `get(30, bold=True)` :66 | 30 | **UI** | **yes (real)** | `segoeuib.ttf` | 40 | 33 | −7 | 40 |
| `body` | `get(21)` :67 | 21 | UI | no | `segoeuil.ttf` | 28 | 23 | −5 | 28 |
| `small` | `get(17)` :68 | 17 | UI | no | `segoeuil.ttf` | 23 | 19 | −4 | 23 |
| `tiny` | `get(14)` :69 | 14 | UI | no | `segoeuil.ttf` | 19 | 16 | −3 | 19 |
| `mono` | `mono_at(16)` :70 | 16 | mono | no | `consola.ttf` | 16 | 12 | −4 | 19 |
| `mono_small` | `mono_at(13)` :71 | 13 | mono | no | `consola.ttf` | 13 | 10 | −3 | 16 |

Note the metric asymmetry, which matters for every layout that vertically centres by height:
Bahnschrift's `hhea` box equals the requested px size exactly (96 → 96), while Segoe UI Light's is
~1.35× it (17 → 23). A different display face changes this a lot — `impact` at 96 reports height
**118**, `arialblack` **136**, `dejavusans` **112**. §0.6.4 says what to do about it.

**Two roles are effectively dead code.** `fonts.mono` (16) has **zero** call sites — grep for
`\.mono\b` across `snake/**/*.py` returns only its own definition. `fonts.mono_small` has exactly
one (`main.py:436`, the FPS readout), gated on `C.SHOW_FPS`, which is `False` in
`snake/config.py:232` and `false` in `web/src/data/config.json`. That readout is also drawn onto
`self.screen` in *window* space after `_blit_to_window()`, i.e. outside the 1280×720 design space and
outside the post-processing chain. Port the mono roles for completeness (they are two lines) but do
not wire them into the design-space scene graph; the web FPS counter belongs in a DOM overlay.

#### 0.3.1 The builder and its cache (fonts.py:74-96)

```
key = (face or "", size, bold)          # ← "" is the no-face-found bucket
cached = self._cache.get(key) -> return
if face:  try SysFont(face, size, bold=bold)  except -> None
if font is None:  font = pygame.font.Font(None, int(size * 1.15));  if bold: font.set_bold(True)
self._cache[key] = font                 # unbounded, never evicted, never cleared
```

The cache is **unbounded and permanent**. That is safe here because the size set is finite
(§0.5) but it is load-bearing for a different reason: several downstream caches are keyed on
`id(font)` — `ui.py:256` `_TEXT_CACHE[(text, id(font), col)]`, `ui.py:766` `_ADVANCE_CACHE[id(font)]`,
`menu.py:196` `_BADGE_CACHE[(label, col, id(font))]`, `mode_select.py:122` and `settings.py` wrap
caches. **The port's `FontBook` must therefore return a stable, shared object per (role, size, bold)**,
not a fresh options literal per call, or every one of those caches degenerates into a per-frame miss.

Consequence worth knowing: if the display face and the UI face ever resolve to the *same* string
(e.g. a system with only `verdana`, where both lists fall through to it), `display_at(30)` and
`get(30, True)` return **the same Font object** and their `id()`-keyed cache entries merge. Harmless,
but it explains why identity keys are safe in Python and why the TS mirror must be identity-stable.

---

### 0.4 The fallback branch, and what it says about the px sizes

`fonts.py:88` — `pygame.font.Font(None, int(size * 1.15))`. `None` means pygame's bundled default,
`pygame.font.get_default_font()` → **`freesansbold.ttf`**: a single always-bold face. So on the
fallback path a non-bold request still comes out bold-ish, and `set_bold(True)` on top double-bolds.

The 1.15 correction exists because the bundled face renders far smaller than the nominal size, and it
is **not enough**: measured, `Font(None, int(21*1.15)) = Font(None, 24)` reports `get_height()` **16**
and `size("SCORE")` **(58, 16)**, against `SysFont("segoeui", 21)`'s **28** and **(61, 28)**. The
fallback is 43% shorter than the face the ladder was tuned for.

**Therefore: every px number in §0.3 and §0.5 is calibrated for a *named system face*, and the port
maps onto the named-face branch, never the 1.15× one.** Do not carry `× 1.15` into TypeScript in any
form. pygame's size argument is the em size in pixels (FreeType at 72 dpi, 1 pt = 1 px), which is
exactly what CSS `font-size: Npx` means, so the ladder transfers **1:1** with no conversion factor.

Three further copies of the same fallback exist and are all unreachable in a healthy run — they only
fire when `game.fonts` is missing or raises. Do not port them as font logic:

| Copy | Lines | Reached when |
|---|---|---|
| `ui.py::_sys_font` | 110-123 (+ `draw_text`'s `_sys_font(18)` default, ui.py:279) | `fonts` is `None`/broken, or `draw_text` is handed a non-`Font` |
| `story_scene.py::_fallback_font` | 394-406 | `_font()` raised |
| `_font` shims returning `None` (`settings.py:1160`, `mode_select.py:362`, `pause.py:411`) | — | then `draw_text` falls through to `_sys_font(18)` |

Port equivalent: a single `FontBook.fallback` = the `body` style. A missing style must degrade to a
*known role of the same stack*, never to a different family.

---

### 0.5 Inventory of every font role and size the game actually requests

#### 0.5.1 The five wrapper shims (and the quirk they hide)

No scene calls `FontBook` directly for named roles; five wrappers sit in between, and their
name-vs-size precedence differs. All are `try`-wrapped and degrade rather than raise.

| Wrapper | Signature | `"display"` | `"ui"` | any other name | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ui.py::_font` :126-138 | `(fonts, name, size=18, bold=False)` | `display_at(size)` | `get(size, bold)` | `getattr(fonts, name)` — **`size` ignored** | falls back to `_sys_font(size, bold)` |
| `settings.py::_font` :1160-1175 | `(name, size=18, bold=False)` | `display_at(size)` | `get(size, bold)` | attr, else `get(size, bold)` | returns `None` if no FontBook |
| `mode_select.py::_font` :362-372 | `(name, size=0)` | `display_at(size)` **iff `size>0`** | `get(size, True)` iff `size>0` | attr | `size=0` default → always the named attr |
| `pause.py::_font` :411-419 | `(fonts, name, size)` | `display_at(size)` | *(no `"ui"` branch)* | attr, else `get(size)` | |
| `story_scene.py::_font` :409-417 | `(fonts, size, *, display=False, bold=False)` | `display_at(int(size))` | — | `get(int(size), bold)` | size-only; no names at all |

**Ported as a quirk — the `size` argument is dead whenever the `name` resolves.** Two call sites are
visibly affected:

| Call site | Asks for | Actually renders | Delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui.py:950` `_font(fonts, "h2", 26, True)` — HUD combo badge `xN` | 26 px | **`h2` = 30 px** Segoe UI Bold | +4 px |
| `pause.py:384` `_font(fonts, "body", 22)` — pause caption | 22 px | **`body` = 21 px** Segoe UI Light | −1 px |

The remainder are consistent with the role they name (`_font(fonts,"tiny",14)`,
`settings._font("h1",42)`, `settings._font("h2",30,True)`, `ui.py:497/499/500`, `ui.py:585`) and can
be ported as plain role references. Reproduce the two above at the size that *ships*, not the size
that is asked for.

#### 0.5.2 Display face (always bold) — every requested size

| px | Requested at | What it draws |
|---|---|---|
| **112** | `story_scene.py:711` | chapter-card roman numeral |
| **96** = `huge` | `menu.py:652` (wordmark), `gameplay.py:1398` (READY countdown digit), `gameplay.py:1415` (`GO!`), `gameover.py:793` (`GAME OVER`), `gameover.py:1093` (`LEVEL CLEAR`) | |
| **74** | `pause.py:360` | `PAUSED` title |
| **64** = `title` | `gameplay._fit_font` tier 1 (:1326), `gameover.py:1088` (`CAMPAIGN COMPLETE`) | |
| **62, 54, 46, 40** | `story_scene.py:696-698` non-chapter title ladder `(size, −8, −16, −22)`, floored by `max(24, trial)` | 4 sizes per card, first that fits `_TEXT_W = 940` |
| **58, 50, 42, 36** | same loop with `size = 58` when `card.is_chapter` | |
| **58** | `gameover.py:1114` | victory score readout |
| **42** = `h1` | `gameplay._fit_font` tier 2, `help_scene.py:534` (`HOW TO PLAY`), `settings.py:854` (`SETTINGS`), `ui.py:585` (tile-button icon glyph) | |
| **34** | `ui.py:1052` (HUD odometer), `mode_select.py:729/811/892` (screen headings) | |
| **32** | `level_select.py:838, 933` | card level numbers |
| **30** | `level_select.py:681` | `SELECT A LEVEL` |
| **26…39** (14 distinct) | `gameover.py:856` `display_at(max(12, int(38 * scale)))` where `scale = 0.7 + 0.3·ease_out_back(pop)` | the `NEW BEST` badge — see below |

The `NEW BEST` badge is the strongest argument for an on-demand cache. `ease_out_back`
(`core/contracts.py:207-211`, `c1=1.70158, c3=2.70158`) peaks at **1.10000405** near `pop≈0.58`, so
`scale ∈ [0.70, 1.03001]` and `int(38·scale)` sweeps **{26,27,…,39}** — fourteen distinct display
fonts built over a 0.45 s animation, one per size, every one permanently cached. A Pixi `TextStyle`
plus its `CanvasTextMetrics` font-metrics probe is comparably expensive to rebuild (it allocates a
canvas and runs a measurement pass), and this is a **draw path**. The TS `FontBook` needs the same
`displayAt(n)` / `get(n, bold)` / `monoAt(n)` on-demand API and the same never-evicted cache.

| **16** | `level_select.py:732` difficulty-tile label |

`level_select.py:291-299` is worth a note: `_bind_fonts()` assigns `self.back.font =
fonts.get(18, bold=True)` once and sets `_fonts_bound = True` **even on failure** ("never retry a
broken FontBook per frame"). The port's equivalent is trivial — styles are cheap — but keep the
one-shot semantics if you mirror the structure.

#### 0.5.5 Distinct request set, summarised

- display (bold): `{112, 96, 74, 64, 62, 58, 54, 50, 46, 42, 40, 36, 34, 32, 30, 26..39}` → **26 keys**
- UI non-bold: `{27, 25, 21, 20, 18, 17, 16, 15, 14}` (+22 requested, never built) → **9 keys**
- UI bold: `{30, 26, 24, 21, 20, 19, 18, 17, 16}` → **9 keys**
- mono: `{16, 13}` → **2 keys**

≈ **46 cache entries** for the whole game, steady state. A `Map<string, TextStyle>` is the right
size of solution; nothing needs eviction, and `FontBook.clear()` exists only for hot-reload.

---

### 0.6 Text measurement

`fonts.py:111-115` defines `FontBook.measure(font, text) -> (w, h)` wrapping `font.size(text)`,
returning `(0, 0)` on failure. **It has zero call sites** — grep `\.measure` across `snake/**/*.py`
finds only the definition. Every measuring site calls `font.size(text)` directly. The port must
implement `measure()` for real anyway, because a Pixi `TextStyle` has no `.size()` and the
alternatives (constructing a throwaway `Text` and reading `.width`) allocate a texture.

#### 0.6.1 Every place the Python measures text

| Site | Expression | Why it measures | Layout kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui.py:771` `_digit_advance` | `max(font.size(d)[0] for d in "0123456789")` | fixed digit pitch so the HUD score cannot jitter | **packs** |
| `ui.py:808` `_draw_odometer` | `oy = int(-roll * font.get_height() * 0.22)` | roll-in travel distance | **metric-scaled animation** |
| `ui.py:564, 587` | `label_y − surf.get_height() * 0.5` | vertical centring of button label / icon | centres |
| `gameover.py:198` `_draw_badge` | `tw, th = font.size(text)`; `w, h = tw + 40, th + 12`; `radius = h // 2`; text at `rect.y + 6` | difficulty-chip geometry from text extent (font supplied at `gameover.py:668` = `small`) | **packs** |
| `menu.py:204` `_badge_surface` | `text_w, text_h = font.size(label)`; `w = text_w + 30`; `h = max(24, text_h + 12)`; text at `(h − text_h) * 0.5` | difficulty pill geometry | **packs** |
| `menu.py:163-168` `_TitleArt` | `base = font.render(TITLE_TEXT)`; if `base.get_width() > TITLE_MAX_W` (= `1280 − 200` = **1080**) then `smoothscale` down by `1080 / w` | wordmark shrink-to-fit | **packs** |
| `menu.py:751, 762` | `rect.centery − badge.get_height() * 0.5` | pill centring | centres |
| `gameplay.py:1330` `_fit_font` | `font.size(text)[0] <= max(40, width)` over `("title","h1","h2")` | largest-that-fits headline | **packs** |
| `gameplay.py:1300` | `pop.y − body.get_height() * 0.5` | popup centring | centres |
| `gameplay.py:1424` | `arena.centery − body.get_height() * 0.5` | `GO!` centring | centres |
| `level_select.py:158` `_wrap.fits` | `font.size(s)[0] <= width` | greedy word wrap, `max_lines`, truncation marker `rstrip(" .,") + "..."` (:181) | **packs** |
| `level_select.py:734` | `r.centery − font.get_height() * 0.5` | tile label centring | centres |
| `level_select.py:890` | `w = font.size(label)[0] + 14` (height fixed at 17) | state tag pill | **packs** |
| `mode_select.py:132` `_wrap` | `font.size(trial)[0] > width` | wrap, cached in `_WRAP_CACHE` keyed on `(text, id(font), width, max_lines)` (:122) | **packs** |
| `settings.py:160` `_wrap` | `font.size(candidate)[0] <= max_w` | wrap | **packs** |
| `story_scene.py:449, 459` `_wrap` | `font.size(text)[0] <= max_w` | wrap to `_TEXT_W = 940` | **packs** |
| `story_scene.py:472-486` `_measure` | `font.size(text[:i+1])[0]` **for every prefix** | per-character x advances driving the typewriter reveal | **packs, O(n²) calls** |
| `story_scene.py:700` | `title_font.size(card.title)[0] <= _TEXT_W` | title ladder (§0.5.2) | **packs** |
| `story_scene.py:1107` | `height = line.body.get_height()` | typewriter clip height | centres/clips |

Wrap widths, for the record: `level_select.py:789` `BAND_W − 28` = **140** (`BAND_W = 168`, :66);
`level_select.py:1045` `hint_w = DETAIL_RECT.right − 22 − hint_x` = `1232 − 22 − 712` = **498**
(`DETAIL_RECT = (48, 586, 1184, 98)`, :75); `mode_select.py:992` `rect.w − 32` = **249**
(`TILE_W = (1280 − 108 − 48) // 4 = 281`, :85); `settings.py:991` `panel.w − 40` = **328**
(`_PREVIEW_W = 368`, :76); `story_scene.py:722` `_TEXT_W` = **940** (:69);
`gameplay.py:1383` `panel.w − 56` = **664** (READY panel is 720 wide).

#### 0.6.2 Reference metrics from the ground-truth machine

Measured with pygame 2.6.1 on the box that produced `E:/SnakeGame/captures/*.png`
(Bahnschrift / Segoe UI Light / Segoe UI Bold / Consolas). These are the numbers the browser has to
be compared against; they are **not** targets to hardcode except where noted.

| Role / px | String | pygame `(w, h)` |
|---|---|---|
| `huge` 96 | `NEON SERPENT` | (699, 96) |
| `huge` 96 | `GAME OVER` | (522, 96) |
| `huge` 96 | `LEVEL CLEAR` | (606, 96) |
| `huge` 96 | `GO!` | (149, 96) |
| `huge` 96 | `3` | (54, 96) |
| `title` 64 | `CAMPAIGN COMPLETE` | (645, 64) |
| `h1` 42 | `SETTINGS` / `HOW TO PLAY` | (190, 42) / (262, 42) |
| `h2` 30 | `PLAY` / `NEW BEST` / `x8` | (71, 40) / (148, 40) / (34, 40) |
| `body` 21 | `HOW TO PLAY` | (130, 28) |
| `small` 17 | `SCORE` / `LEVEL 01` / `GOAL  12 ORBS` / `CLICK THE CARD TO PLAY` | (49, 23) / (64, 23) / (111, 23) / (191, 23) |
| `tiny` 14 | `SCORE` / `LIVES` / `BOOST` / `COMBO` / `BEST 12,345` / `NO ACTIVE POWER-UPS` | (41, 19) / (31, 19) / (44, 19) / (50, 19) / (69, 19) / (148, 19) |
| `tiny` 14 | `NEW` / `CLEARED` / `PERFECT` / `x2` / `v1.0` | (30, 19) / (55, 19) / (53, 19) / (13, 19) / (21, 19) |
| `mono_small` 13 | `" 60.0 fps"` | (63, 13) |

**Two of these are load-bearing and should be ported as constants, not re-measured**, because they
size a container rather than position glyphs:

1. `_digit_advance(display_at(34))` — Bahnschrift 34 bold digit widths are
   `{0:20, 1:12, 2:19, 3:19, 4:21, 5:20, 6:19, 7:18, 8:20, 9:19}`, max = **21**, and `","` = 9.
   So the HUD odometer step is `adv = 21` for digits and `max(4, 21 // 2) = 10` for the comma
   (`ui.py:804`). Hardcode 21/10, or the score readout's total width changes with the browser's face
   and the `BEST …` caption above it no longer lines up.
2. `_draw_odometer`'s roll travel, `0.22 * font.get_height()` with `get_height() = 34` →
   **7.48 px**. Pixi's `fontProperties.fontSize` for the same face is the canvas ascent+descent and
   will not be 34. Hardcode 7.48 (or `0.22 * 34`), and keep the comment: the travel is deliberately
   short so a rolling digit cannot climb into the `SCORE` caption.

Badge/pill heights follow from the `th` column and are equally worth freezing:
`_draw_badge` with `small` → `h = 23 + 12 = 35`, `radius = 17`; `menu._badge_surface` with `tiny` →
`h = max(24, 19 + 12) = 31`, text at `y = (31 − 19) * 0.5 = 6`.

#### 0.6.3 `_fit_font` resolved for the shipped content

Width budget 664 px. All twelve level names (longest: `EMERALD CIRCUIT` at 540) take tier 1
(`title`, 64). Every story beat title overflows it and takes tier 2 (`h1`, 42):

| Headline | `title` 64 | `h1` 42 | `h2` 30 | chosen |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `EMERALD CIRCUIT` | 540 | 354 | 264 | `title` |
| `CRIMSON ENGINE` | 516 | 337 | 261 | `title` |
| `EVERYTHING, REFRACTED` | 750 | 490 | 369 | `h1` |
| `SOMETHING IN THE TRENCH` | 814 | 533 | 416 | `h1` |
| `RIBBONS OVER A QUIET SEA` | 828 | 544 | 407 | `h1` |
| `THE MACHINE WANTS FEEDING` | 906 | 596 | 456 | `h1` |
| `THE LAST LIGHT BENDS INWARD` | 946 | 621 | 471 | `h1` |

Tier 3 (`h2`) is never reached by shipped content. Useful as a regression assertion: if the web
build ever picks `h2` for a level name, the display stack has fallen through to something much wider
than Bahnschrift.

#### 0.6.4 The Pixi equivalent, and its hazards

`CanvasTextMetrics.measureText(text, style, canvas?, wordWrap?)`
(`pixi.js/lib/scene/text/canvas/CanvasTextMetrics.d.ts:153`) returns `{ width, height, lines,
lineWidths, lineHeight, maxLineWidth, fontProperties }`. Note the signature takes a **`TextStyle`
instance, not `TextStyleOptions`** — which is the second reason `FontBook` must cache a `TextStyle`
per key alongside the options it hands out (§0.7).

- **`w` maps to `metrics.width`; `h` maps to `metrics.height`.** For single-line text with
  `wordWrap: false` these correspond to `font.size(text)`. Pass `wordWrap = false` explicitly —
  Python never uses engine wrapping.
- **Do not put `dropShadow` in a measured style.** `CanvasTextMetrics._adjustWidthForStyle`
  (:160) inflates the returned width for stroke and drop shadow. `draw_text` (`ui.py:268-291`, shadow blit at :287) draws
  its shadow as a *separate blit* of a black silhouette at `(+2, +2)` with `set_alpha(150)`, and
  `font.size()` therefore reports the unshadowed extent. If the port expresses the shadow as a
  `TextStyle.dropShadow`, `_fit_font` steps down a tier too eagerly and every measured pill grows.
  Keep the shadow as a second `Text` (the ui-kit section owns that decision; this section only
  requires that the *measured* style carry no shadow and no stroke).
- **`letterSpacing` must be 0.** Python applies none. The existing placeholder (§0.7) sets 1 and 2;
  those are inventions and must go, or every measured width drifts by `n_chars` px.
- **Metrics differ between pygame's face and the browser's, always.** Even with the identical font
  file, pygame reports `hhea`-derived heights while Pixi reports canvas-probed ascent+descent, and
  hinting/subpixel rules differ. Rule of thumb for the verifier:
  - Layouts that **centre** measured text are safe — port them to `anchor.set(0.5)` /
    `anchor.set(0.5, 0)` and the measurement disappears entirely. Every `get_height() * 0.5` site in
    §0.6.1 is one of these; none of them should call `measure()` in TypeScript.
  - Layouts that **pack** against measured widths need a look at the capture: `_digit_advance`,
    `_draw_badge`, `menu._badge_surface`, `level_select`'s state tag, `_fit_font`, `_TitleArt`'s
    1080 px shrink, and all five `_wrap` helpers. Wrapping is the highest-risk of these — a 4% width
    difference moves one word across a line break and changes the visible line count.
  - `draw_text`'s `pos[1]` is **always the top edge** (`ui.py:274`; centre/right offsets at :283-285), and `pos[0]` is
    left/centre/right per `align`. That is `anchor = (0 | 0.5 | 1, 0)`. Getting this one mapping
    right removes most of the metric exposure in the whole UI.
- **`story_scene._measure` is a prefix loop.** Keep the prefix semantics (kerning means per-glyph
  widths do not sum correctly), but do it with one shared `CanvasRenderingContext2D` and
  `ctx.measureText(prefix).width` rather than `CanvasTextMetrics.measureText` per prefix — the latter
  re-probes font properties each call. It runs once per story card in `_build_layout`, not per frame.

---

### 0.7 The TypeScript design — `web/src/gfx/fonts.ts`

```ts
export type FontRole = "display" | "ui" | "mono";

/** CSS stacks — the port of DISPLAY_FACES / UI_FACES / MONO_FACES (§0.2, §0.8). */
export const DISPLAY_STACK: string;
export const UI_STACK: string;
export const MONO_STACK: string;

export interface Metrics { readonly w: number; readonly h: number; }

export class FontBook {
  // The ladder, fonts.py:63-71. Options objects, frozen, identity-stable.
  readonly huge: TextStyleOptions;       // display 96
  readonly title: TextStyleOptions;      // display 64
  readonly h1: TextStyleOptions;         // display 42
  readonly h2: TextStyleOptions;         // ui 30 bold
  readonly body: TextStyleOptions;       // ui 21
  readonly small: TextStyleOptions;      // ui 17
  readonly tiny: TextStyleOptions;       // ui 14
  readonly mono: TextStyleOptions;       // mono 16   (no call sites; ported for parity)
  readonly monoSmall: TextStyleOptions;  // mono 13   (FPS overlay only)

  /** fonts.get(size, bold=False) — UI face. */
  get(size: number, bold?: boolean): TextStyleOptions;
  /** fonts.display_at(size) — display face, ALWAYS bold. */
  displayAt(size: number): TextStyleOptions;
  /** fonts.mono_at(size, bold=False). */
  monoAt(size: number, bold?: boolean): TextStyleOptions;

  /** The shared TextStyle behind an options object — for measurement and for `new Text`. */
  style(opts: TextStyleOptions): TextStyle;
  /** fonts.measure / font.size(text). wordWrap forced off. */
  measure(opts: TextStyleOptions, text: string): Metrics;
  measureWidth(opts: TextStyleOptions, text: string): number;

  /** Raster sharpness under the design-space upscale — see below. Call from applyResize. */
  setResolution(r: number): void;
  clear(): void;   // hot-reload only
}
```

Design points, each tied to a decision already settled elsewhere in `docs/port/`:

1. **Hands out `TextStyleOptions`, not `Text`.** Mirrors `FontBook` handing out `Font`, not
   surfaces. Callers do `new Text({ text, style: fonts.style(fonts.small) })` and keep the object
   across frames (`GameplayScene` already pools popup `Text`s and only assigns `.text` when the
   bound popup changes — that is the correct pattern, keep it everywhere).
2. **Cache keyed on `(role, size, bold)`** — the exact triple of `fonts.py:75`, with `role`
   replacing the resolved face name (the browser resolves the face, so there is nothing to key on).
   One `Map<string, { opts: TextStyleOptions; style: TextStyle }>`, never evicted (~46 entries,
   §0.5.5). Identity stability is required by §0.3.1.
3. **Colour leaves the style.** Every cached style has `fill: 0xffffff`; colour arrives as
   `text.tint = toHex(rgb)` and intensity as `text.alpha` — the same rule `web/src/gfx/textures.ts`
   states for glow textures, and here it buys something extra: Python's `_TEXT_CACHE`
   (`ui.py:257`) is keyed on `(text, id(font), colour)` and so rebuilds the raster for every colour,
   while a white-filled `Text` + `tint` reuses one raster across all colours and across the
   per-frame `lerp_color`/`pulse` animation that most labels apply. The one exception is the same
   one as for textures: `shade(c, f)` with `f > 1` (e.g. `P.shade(theme.text_dim, 1.05)` at
   `help_scene.py:600`, `mode_select.py:816/897`) saturates per channel toward white and cannot be
   expressed as tint × alpha — compute the resulting RGB in TS via `palette.shade` and tint with
   *that*, which is exact because `shade` is already ported.
4. **`fontWeight` is where the two Windows quirks land.** Non-bold UI roles → `"300"`
   (Segoe UI Light, §0.2.1). Bold UI roles → `"700"`. Display roles → see §0.8.
5. **`Text.resolution` under the design-space upscale.** `Game.init` sets renderer
   `resolution: min(devicePixelRatio, 3)` (`web/src/app/Game.ts:110`), and `Viewport` then scales the
   whole world by `scale = min(w/1280, h/720)` (`Viewport.ts:61`). A `Text` rasterises at its style's
   `fontSize × resolution` and is then magnified by the world transform, so at a 1920-wide window on
   a 1× display a 96 px headline is rasterised at 96 px and blown up 1.5× — soft, and glaringly so on
   `huge`. Set `text.resolution = min(3, max(1, viewport.scale * rendererResolution))` and re-apply
   from `Game.applyResize` via `FontBook.setResolution`. (`resolution` is a settable per-`Text`
   property in v8 — `pixi.js/lib/scene/text/AbstractText.d.ts:175, 310`; `null` means auto.) This has
   no pygame counterpart: the Python canvas *is* the design space and `main.py:455` scales the
   finished frame once, so its text is upscaled too — but the Python is upscaling a 1280-wide
   composite, which is the look the captures show. Sharper text is an acceptable, and desirable,
   divergence; blurry text is not.
6. **No engine word-wrap.** `wordWrap: false` on every style. The five Python `_wrap` helpers have
   distinct behaviours (`max_lines`, the `rstrip(" .,") + "..."` ellipsis at `level_select.py:180`,
   the `[:3]` / `[:9]` line caps) that Pixi's greedy wrapper does not reproduce. Port them as TS
   functions that call `fonts.measureWidth`.
7. **What this replaces.** `web/src/scenes/GameplayScene.ts:58-70` currently holds a placeholder:

   ```ts
   const POPUP_STYLE: TextStyleOptions = {
     fontFamily: "system-ui, sans-serif", fontSize: 20, fontWeight: "700",
     fill: 0xffffff, letterSpacing: 1,
   };
   const POPUP_STYLE_BIG: TextStyleOptions = { ...POPUP_STYLE, fontSize: 30, letterSpacing: 2 };
   ```

   Three things are wrong against `gameplay.py:1293` (`font = fonts.h2 if pop.big else fonts.small`):
   the small popup is **17 px, weight 300, Segoe UI** — not 20 px / 700 / `system-ui`; the big popup
   is right at 30 px and 700 but must be the UI stack, not `system-ui`; and `letterSpacing` is an
   invention (§0.6.4). Replace both with `fonts.small` and `fonts.h2`. The `fill: 0xffffff` +
   `text.tint = toHex(p.color)` pattern already there is correct — keep it.
8. **Names already promised.** `docs/port/integration.md` §9 spells the READY/GO card in terms of
   `fonts.small`, `fonts.tiny`, `fonts.title`/`h1`/`h2` (the `_fit_font` ladder), `fonts.body` and
   `fonts.huge`; §11 lists `game.fonts` with "faces used here: `tiny, small, body, h2, h1, title,
   huge` (+ `mono_small` shell-side FPS)". The names above match that spec exactly — do not rename,
   and note the only camel-casing is `monoSmall` for Python's `mono_small`.
9. **Optional, not required:** the HUD odometer draws at most ~10 glyphs from `{0-9 , }` at
   display 34, every frame, with per-glyph alpha and offset. A `BitmapFont.install` over that glyph
   set would turn it into one batched draw. Treat as an optimisation after parity, and only if the
   frame budget asks for it — a pooled `Text` per character slot (which is what Python effectively
   has, via `_TEXT_CACHE`) is the faithful and simpler first version.

---

### 0.8 Web-specific risks, and the stacks to ship

#### 0.8.1 Font loading is asynchronous even for system faces

Chrome resolves a *system* family synchronously in most cases, but `document.fonts` is still a
`FontFaceSet` with a lifecycle, and the first `Text` constructed before layout has settled can
measure against a fallback face and **latch the wrong width** — Pixi caches the measurement with the
raster, so a wrongly-measured pill or a wrongly-tiered `_fit_font` headline stays wrong until the
text changes. Two mitigations, use both:

- `await document.fonts.ready` in `Game.init` immediately after `await this.app.init(...)`
  (`web/src/app/Game.ts:107-121`) and before the first scene is pushed. `init` is already `async`, so
  this costs one line and no restructuring. Guard it for the headless branch (`Game.ts:98-101`
  returns early before touching the DOM).
- **Measure lazily, at draw time, never in a constructor or module top level.** The current
  placeholder is a module-level `const` object, which is fine because it is only *options*; the
  hazard appears the moment a module-level `TextStyle` or `Text` is created. `FontBook` must build
  styles on first `get()`, which the cache-on-demand design gives for free.

#### 0.8.2 Which faces each platform will actually hit

| Platform | display | UI (non-bold / bold) | mono |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Windows 10 1709+ / 11** | `Bahnschrift` — the capture face | `Segoe UI` Light 300 / Bold 700 — the capture faces | `Consolas` |
| Windows 8.1 and older | `Impact` (no Bahnschrift) | `Segoe UI` | `Consolas` |
| **macOS** | `Impact` | `Helvetica Neue` (has a real Light) | `Menlo` |
| **iOS / iPadOS** | `Impact` is **not** guaranteed on iOS; expect the stack to fall through | `Helvetica Neue` | `Menlo`, else `Courier New` |
| **Android (Chrome)** | none of the eight exist; Chrome aliases `Arial`/`Helvetica` → **`Roboto`** | `Roboto` (Light exists) | `monospace` → Roboto Mono / Droid Sans Mono |
| Linux desktop | `DejaVu Sans` (or `Liberation`-mapped `Arial`) | `DejaVu Sans` | `DejaVu Sans Mono` |

The display face is where the divergence bites, because the eight candidates are wildly different
widths and heights at the same px size. Measured, `"NEON SERPENT"` at 96 px bold:

| face | width | `get_height()` |
|---|---|---|
| `bahnschrift` (Windows, **the capture**) | 699 | 96 |
| `franklingothicheavy` | 697 | 109 |
| `impact` (macOS) | **548** | 118 |
| `dejavusans` | 831 | 112 |
| `verdana` | 834 | 117 |
| `arialblack` | 848 | 136 |

So a macOS wordmark is 22% narrower than a Windows one and 23% taller — with the same code and the
same numbers. `_TitleArt`'s shrink threshold (1080 px) is not tripped by any of them, so nothing
clamps it. **This is inherent to the original design and must not be "fixed" by hardcoding widths**;
it is why every display-face layout in the game centres rather than packs. Verify per platform
against the captures, and treat any *packing* against a display face as a bug in the port.

#### 0.8.3 Recommended stacks

Every stack ends in a generic that always exists. `system-ui` deliberately does **not** appear first
anywhere: on Windows it is Segoe UI Regular, which would silently overwrite both the display face
and the Light weight the captures were made with.

```ts
export const DISPLAY_STACK =
  'Bahnschrift, Impact, "Franklin Gothic Heavy", "Arial Black", ' +
  '"Helvetica Neue", "DejaVu Sans", Verdana, Arial, system-ui, sans-serif';

export const UI_STACK =
  '"Segoe UI", Selawik, "Helvetica Neue", Roboto, "DejaVu Sans", ' +
  'Verdana, Arial, system-ui, sans-serif';

export const MONO_STACK =
  'Consolas, "Cascadia Mono", Menlo, "DejaVu Sans Mono", ' +
  '"Liberation Mono", "Courier New", monospace';
```

Weights per role:

| Role group | `fontFamily` | `fontWeight` | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `huge`, `title`, `h1`, every `displayAt(n)` | `DISPLAY_STACK` | **`"700"`**, verify against captures | pygame *asks* for bold (`display_at` always passes `bold=True`), and four of the eight candidate faces (`impact`, `franklingothicheavy`, `arialblack`, plus Roboto-via-`Arial` on Android) are inherently heavy with no bold cut, so 700 is what the design means. The one caveat: on Windows the browser will instantiate Bahnschrift's variable `wght`=700, whereas pygame fake-bolded `wght`=400 (+1.3% width at 96 px). If `huge`/`title` read fatter than `captures/01-menu.png` or `captures/08-gameplay-ready.png`, step down to `600` then `500` — do not compensate by changing `fontSize`. |
| `h2` and every `get(n, true)` | `UI_STACK` | `"700"` | real Segoe UI Bold in the capture; every fallback family has a real 700 |
| `body`, `small`, `tiny`, every `get(n)` | `UI_STACK` | **`"300"`** | the Segoe UI Light quirk (§0.2.1); Helvetica Neue Light and Roboto Light exist, so the intent carries to macOS/iOS/Android |
| `mono`, `monoSmall`, `monoAt(n)` | `MONO_STACK` | `"400"` / `"700"` when `bold` | Consolas has real regular and bold |

Also set on every style, for parity: `wordWrap: false`, `letterSpacing: 0`, `align: "left"`, no
`stroke`, no `dropShadow`, `fill: 0xffffff`, `padding: 0`. `fontStyle` is always `"normal"` — nothing
in the game ever asks for italics (`Sysfonts`' italic slots are never touched).

#### 0.8.4 What the screenshot harness does and does not prove

`web/tools/shot.mjs` boots the build in headless Chrome on this same Windows box, so its screenshots
resolve to Bahnschrift / Segoe UI Light / Consolas — the identical faces the Python captures used.
That makes per-pixel-adjacent perceptual comparison against `E:/SnakeGame/captures/*.png` meaningful
for *this* platform, and proves nothing whatsoever about iOS or Android. Any layout in the "packs"
column of §0.6.1 needs a second look on a real phone before the Capacitor wrapper ships.

---

### 0.9 Quirk checklist for this section

Copy each of these; none is a bug to fix.

1. `fonts.get(n)` non-bold is **Segoe UI Light (weight 300)** on Windows, because pygame's family
   normaliser strips `Light` and that file wins the `(False, False)` slot (`Sysfonts["segoeui"]`).
   → CSS `font-weight: 300`.
2. Display bold is **synthetic** (`bahnschrift.ttf` has no bold entry; `set_bold(True)`), +1.3% width
   at 96 px, much lighter than a real 700 cut.
3. `_font(fonts, "h2", 26, True)` (`ui.py:950`, HUD combo badge) renders at **30 px**, not 26 — the
   shim ignores `size` when the name resolves.
4. `_font(fonts, "body", 22)` (`pause.py:384`) renders at **21 px**, not 22, for the same reason.
5. `FontBook.measure` has **no call sites** in Python; the port needs it regardless.
6. `fonts.mono` (16 px) is **built and never used**; `fonts.mono_small` is used only by the
   `SHOW_FPS = False` overlay, drawn in window space outside the design space and the post chain.
7. `gameover.py:856` builds **fourteen** distinct display fonts (26…39 px) during one 0.45 s badge
   animation — the on-demand cache is not optional.
8. `story_scene.py:696` builds the first title font twice (once before the ladder loop, once as the
   loop's first iteration) — a cache hit, harmless, and worth not "tidying" if you mirror the
   structure.
9. The 1.15× fallback (`Font(None, int(size * 1.15))`) renders in `freesansbold.ttf` and is still
   ~43% shorter than the named face it stands in for. It is unreachable on any target platform. Do
   not port the factor.
10. `pygame.font.get_fonts()`'s `arial` entry is **Arial Narrow** (`ARIALN.TTF`) on Windows. Only
    reachable in the deep fallback, but do not "correct" it if a capture ever shows narrow text.
## 1. Module foundations, caches and the local glow layer

Ground truth: `E:/SnakeGame/snake/gfx/ui.py:1-200`. Every line reference below is into that file
unless another is named. Read `docs/port/render.md` §0-§1 first: this section reuses `stampGlow`,
"glow texture cache" and "the additive-glow convention" and never redefines them.

### 1.1 What the module claims to be (docstring, lines 1-21)

> "Mouse-driven UI toolkit... Everything the player actually touches lives here: the animated
> `Button`, the frosted panels behind menus, text with a drop shadow, progress / stamina bars, the
> custom glowing cursor and the whole in-game HUD strip."

The docstring then states the two rules that govern every pixel in the file (lines 8-20):

| # | Rule | Why it exists | Port consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 (10-12) | `pygame.draw.*` does **not** alpha-blend onto an opaque destination - it writes the colour straight in. Anything translucent is composed onto an `SRCALPHA` scratch surface first and blitted afterwards. | pygame's drawing primitives ignore the destination when the colour carries alpha | In Pixi every `Graphics` fill *does* blend, so the scratch surface is unnecessary **except** where the scratch existed to make overlapping strokes share one uniform alpha (same reasoning as render.md §2 `_c`: use one container + `AlphaFilter`, never per-child alpha) |
| R2 (13-16) | Additive glows are blitted with `BLEND_RGB_ADD`, which **ignores the source alpha**, so glow surfaces bake their falloff into the **RGB** channels and live on plain black surfaces (black adds nothing, so the square corners are invisible). | the additive-glow convention, render.md §1 | Identical to render.py. Under "colour leaves the texture" the falloff lives in a **white** texture and colour arrives as `tint`, intensity as `alpha` |

R2 has a corollary the port must not lose: because the glow surfaces carry **no alpha channel at
all** (plain `pygame.Surface`, §1.7/§1.9), blitting one *without* `BLEND_RGB_ADD` paints an opaque
black square. Every call site passes the flag; a Pixi sprite that forgets `blendMode = "add"` will
reproduce the black box faithfully.

Lines 18-19 state the caching intent - *"Both kinds of surface are expensive to build, so every one
of them is cached by shape + colour"* - and lines 19-20 the error policy: **"Nothing in this module
ever raises: a UI that explodes mid frame would take the whole game down with it."**

The module's public surface is `__all__` (38-45): `Button, draw_panel, draw_text, draw_bar,
draw_cursor, draw_hud` - six names, and the TS module boundary should mirror exactly that. Every one
of them swallows: `draw_panel` (try 235 / except 245), `draw_text` (278/290), `draw_bar` (333/351),
`draw_cursor` (595/659), `draw_hud` (976/983 **plus a `finally` with its own nested try at 986-988**
that restores the clip rect), `Button.draw` (503/537), and also the two non-drawing entry points
`Button.update` (452/466) and `Button.handle_event` (471/488, which resets `self._armed` in the
handler). Corrections to what an earlier draft of this section claimed: **`Button._draw_content`
(540) is not wrapped** - it relies on `Button.draw`'s guard, and its only local `try` is the
`rotozoom` at 557-562 - and **`_draw_icon` (573) is not wrapped either**. Private helpers with their
own guards: `_rect` (104-107), `_sys_font` (118-121), `_font` (128-137), `_powerup_types` (683-687),
`_hud_anim` (718-729, `except TypeError` for an unhashable/non-weakref-able game), `_digit_advance`
(770-772), `_draw_effect_icon` (845/877), `_draw_combo`'s badge blit (953-955), and `num()` inside
`_draw_hud_impl` (1000-1003). Per render.md §8 the port drops these swallow-alls (TS has real
types); keep **one** try/catch at the scene root so a thrown UI error cannot kill the ticker, and let
dev builds assert instead.

### 1.2 Imports and the module's entire constant surface (lines 23-60)

| Python (line) | What it is | TS equivalent | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `import math` (25) | `cos/sin` for the cursor spurs and HUD rings | `Math` | - |
| `import weakref` (26) | used **only** by the HUD's per-`Game` animation store: `_HUD_ANIM = weakref.WeakKeyDictionary()` (ui.py:713) read through `_hud_anim` (717-729), with an `id(game)`-keyed `_HUD_ANIM_FALLBACK` (714) for unhashable games | HUD section owns it; in TS the HUD renderer is an object that owns its own animation state - no weak map needed | HUD section |
| `from typing import ...` (27) | `Any, Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple` (+ `TYPE_CHECKING`, guarding the `Game` import at 35-36) | TS types | - |
| `import pygame` (29) | surfaces, rects, fonts, draw | Pixi `Graphics` / `Sprite` / `Text` | - |
| `from .. import config as C` (31) | exactly ten names, and no others (verified by grep): `C.UI_CORNER=12` (config.py:174), `C.UI_CLICK_COOLDOWN=0.10` (177), `C.WINDOW_W=1280`/`WINDOW_H=720` (28-29), `C.HUD_H=78` (63), `C.START_LIVES=3` (159), `C.COMBO_MAX=8` (144), `C.SNAKE_BOOST_MAX=100.0` (81), `C.SNAKE_BOOST_MIN_TO_START=12.0` (82), `C.POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION=7.0` (154) | `web/src/core/config.ts` | nine of ten present - `UI_CORNER` config.ts:142, `WINDOW_W/H` 52-53, `HUD_H` 58, `START_LIVES` 127, `COMBO_MAX` 112, `SNAKE_BOOST_MAX` 74, `SNAKE_BOOST_MIN_TO_START` 75, `POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION` 122. **`UI_CLICK_COOLDOWN` is missing:** the value is in the data (`web/src/data/config.json:122`) but `config.ts` never exports it - the buttons section must add `export const UI_CLICK_COOLDOWN = num("UI_CLICK_COOLDOWN", 0.1);` |
| `from .. import palette as P` (32) | `shade`, `lerp_color`, `with_alpha`, `rainbow`, `theme_for_level`, `Theme`, and the `UI_*` colours | `web/src/core/palette.ts` | **all present**, see below |
| `from ..core.contracts import TAU, clamp, ease_out_back, pulse` (33) | maths | `web/src/core/mathx.ts` - `TAU` (10), `clamp` (12), `easeOutBack` (65), `pulse` (74) | **all present**; `pulse(t, speed=1) = 0.5+0.5·sin(t·speed)` matches contracts.py:214-216 exactly, and `ease_out_back` (contracts.py:207-211, `c1=1.70158, c3=2.70158`) matches mathx.ts:65 |
| `RGB = Tuple[int,int,int]` (47) | type alias | `RGB` (palette.ts:20, a `readonly [number,number,number]`) | present |

**There are no `UI_*` colours defined in ui.py.** They all live in `snake/palette.py:213-221` and are
referenced as `P.UI_*`. Every one is already exported by `web/src/core/palette.ts`, whose values are
loaded from the `ui` block of **`web/src/data/themes.json` (lines 774-812)** - *not* config.json -
with the palette.py literals as fallbacks (`ui()` helper, palette.ts:274-278), so they are
data-driven on both sides:

| Colour | palette.py | Value | palette.ts | in themes.json `ui`? | used by ui.py? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UI_PANEL` | 213 | `(16, 19, 34)` | 281 | yes | yes |
| `UI_PANEL_LIGHT` | 214 | `(28, 33, 56)` | 283 | yes | yes (`_panel_surface` gradient top) |
| `UI_SHADOW` | 215 | `(0, 0, 0)` | 285 (literal - not in the data dump) | **no** | **never** - the drop shadow is a black silhouette built by `_text_pair` (263), not this constant |
| `UI_WHITE` | 216 | `(240, 246, 255)` | 287 | yes | yes |
| `UI_DIM` | 217 | `(132, 146, 176)` | 289 | yes | yes |
| `UI_GOOD` | 218 | `(86, 240, 160)` | 291 | yes | yes |
| `UI_WARN` | 219 | `(255, 196, 72)` | 293 | yes | yes |
| `UI_BAD` | 220 | `(255, 84, 108)` | 295 | yes | yes |
| `UI_GOLD` | 221 | `(255, 208, 84)` | 297 | yes | yes |

The full set of `P.*` names ui.py touches, so the port's palette import list is exact: `Theme`,
`lerp_color`, `shade`, `with_alpha`, `rainbow`, `theme_for_level`, and the eight `UI_*` above.
`P.clamp8` is never called directly - it only acts *inside* `shade`/`lerp_color`/`with_alpha`.

`palette.ts:300-311` also pre-packs all nine as `UI_HEX.{panel,panelLight,shadow,white,dim,good,
warn,bad,gold}` (`0xRRGGBB`) - use those directly as Pixi tints instead of calling `toHex` per frame.
**The port must add nothing to palette.ts for this module.** `UI_WHITE` is *bluish*
`(240,246,255)`, not `#fff`; where the Python wants pure white it says so literally (as
`gfx/entities/hazardGlow.ts:61` already notes). ui.py always uses `P.UI_WHITE`.

The only module-level *numeric* constant in the whole file is the cache limit:

```python
_CACHE_LIMIT = 384                                       # ui.py:53
```

For completeness, the module's other module-level names, all outside lines 1-200 and all owned by
later sections: `_STYLES = ("primary", "ghost", "danger", "tile")` (358, buttons),
`_PU_FALLBACK` (668) and its memo `_PU_TYPES = None` (676) (HUD power-up chips), `_HUD_ANIM` (713) +
`_HUD_ANIM_FALLBACK` (714), and `_ADVANCE_CACHE` (760). There are no other constants: every other
number in the file is a literal at its use site.

### 1.3 The seven declared caches, two undeclared ones, and `_store` (lines 49-68)

```python
def _store(cache, key, value, limit=_CACHE_LIMIT):       # ui.py:63
    """Insert into a cache, wiping it wholesale if it has grown too large."""
    if len(cache) >= limit:
        cache.clear()                                    # ui.py:66  <-- total wipe
    cache[key] = value
    return value
```

**The eviction policy, exactly:** when the dict has reached `limit` entries the *entire dict is
cleared* and the new entry becomes its only member. Not LRU, not FIFO, not "evict one" - a
guillotine. The consequence is a sawtooth: the cache fills to `limit`, drops to 1, refills. Nothing
is ever evicted while the cache is below its limit, so a stale entry (an old theme's colour) lives
forever until the limit trips (comment at ui.py:50-51: *"Keys always contain the shape and the
colour, so a theme change simply starts populating new entries instead of showing stale art."*).
A limit exists at all because the keys embed **colour**: HUD colours are animated (score heat, combo
rainbow, pulsing warnings), so without quantisation *and* a limit the caches would grow without
bound and consume CPU RAM - a `_panel_surface` is `w*h*4` bytes (the 736x520 game-over panel alone
is 1.5 MB).

| Cache | Line | Key | Value | Limit passed at the `_store` call | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_GLOW_CACHE` | 54 | `(radius:int, col:_q8, round(clamp(i,0,3),1))` (154) | `Surface(2r x 2r)` | **512** (167) | `_glow_add` radial blooms |
| `_RGLOW_CACHE` | 55 | `(w, h, corner:int, col:_q8, spread, round(clamp(i,0,2),1))` (186) | `Surface(w+2s, h+2s)` | 384 (198) | `_rrect_glow` panel/button halos |
| `_PANEL_CACHE` | 56 | `(w, h, alpha:int, border:bool, accent, corner:int)` (208) - the accent is `_q`-ed by the *caller* (239), not inside | frosted panel | 384 (229) | `_panel_surface` (panels section) |
| `_TEXT_CACHE` | 57 | `(text, id(font), col:_q8)` (256) | `(glyphs, black silhouette)` | **900** (265) | `_text_pair` (text section) |
| `_BAR_CACHE` | 58 | `("track", w, h, bg, border:bool, edge)` (298) and `("fill", w, h, color)` (312) | `Surface(w x h)`, `SRCALPHA` | 384 (307, 327) | `draw_bar`'s track and its gradient fill (bars section) |
| `_MISC_CACHE` | 59 | three tagged shapes: `("btn", w, h, style, hot:bool, enabled:bool, _q(accent), _q(accent2), _q(text))` (388-389), `("hud", w, h, _q(accent), _q(accent2))` (735), `("chip", size, col)` (882) | `Surface`, `SRCALPHA` | 384 (413, 757, 889) | **button bodies** - the dominant occupant, two per (size, style, enabled, theme) because hover is a cold/hot cross-fade - plus the HUD backdrop strip and the power-up chip backgrounds. Life icons are **not** cached: `_draw_life_icon` (821) draws directly every frame |
| `_FONT_CACHE` | 60 | `(size:int, bold:bool)` (112) | `pygame.font.Font` | **unbounded** - `_sys_font` writes `_FONT_CACHE[key]` directly (122), never via `_store` | the module-local fallback font |

Two further caches live outside the 49-60 block and complete the inventory:

| Cache | Line | Key | Eviction | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_ADVANCE_CACHE` | 760 | `id(font)` (766) | the same guillotine, **hand-written** at 774-776 (`if len(...) >= _CACHE_LIMIT: clear()`) instead of going through `_store` | widest digit advance per font, so the score odometer's columns never jitter (`_digit_advance`, 763-778) |
| `_HUD_ANIM_FALLBACK` | 714 | `id(game)` (725) | **none, ever** | `_HudAnim` for a game object that cannot be weak-referenced. `_HUD_ANIM` (713) is a `WeakKeyDictionary` and self-cleaning; this fallback is not, though with one `Game` per process it never grows |

So the guillotine appears **twice** in the module (`_store` 65-66 and the inline copy at 774-776),
and **two** stores key on a CPython object address (`_TEXT_CACHE` via `id(font)`, `_ADVANCE_CACHE`
directly). Neither may be reproduced; key on a stable font id.

**Policy the port should use, and why it differs.** A pygame surface is CPU RAM owned by the GC; a
Pixi `Texture` is GPU memory that must be `destroy(true)`-ed, and destroying one that a live sprite
still references produces a blank or GL-error-spewing sprite in the *same* frame. So the Python
policy is actively unsafe here: `cache.clear()` mid-frame would drop textures that sprites created
earlier in the same frame are still pointing at.

1. **Do not port `_store` at all.** Under "colour leaves the texture" every remaining key is
   *geometry only*, so each cache is bounded by construction at a few dozen entries (§1.4, §1.7,
   §1.9). `web/src/gfx/textures.ts:562-578` already states this policy for the existing caches:
   *"the caches are bounded by construction (a few dozen radii) and are meant to live for the
   session"*, with `disposeGeneratedTextures()` reserved for tests and hot-reload. Extend that
   module; **do not add a second cache with a second policy.**
2. If a bound is ever wanted, evict by **frame stamp** (drop entries untouched for N frames, at the
   *end* of a frame), never `clear()`.
3. `_TEXT_CACHE` is the one cache whose TS analogue can still grow (arbitrary strings): the text
   section should key on `(text, styleId)` with the colour moved to `tint`, and either use Pixi's own
   `Text` object lifecycle or a frame-stamped map. **Note the `id(font)` quirk** (ui.py:256): the key
   embeds a CPython object address, so a font object that is GC-ed and a new one allocated at the
   same address would serve stale glyphs. It cannot fire in practice (the `FontBook` holds every face
   for the process lifetime) but it must not be reproduced - key on a stable font id.

### 1.4 The quantisers `_q`, `_q8`, `_step` (lines 71-97)

```python
def _q(c):   return (int(c[0]) & 0xFF, int(c[1]) & 0xFF, int(c[2]) & 0xFF)      # ui.py:73
def _q8(c):  return (min(255, (int(c[0]) + 4) // 8 * 8), ...)                    # ui.py:84-86
def _step(v, steps=6): return round(clamp(v, 0.0, 1.0) * steps) / steps          # ui.py:97
```

| Fn | Exact arithmetic | Distinct outputs | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_q` | per channel `int(c) & 0xFF` | 256/channel | **Not a quantiser at all** - it coerces whatever a duck-typed `theme` attribute holds into a hashable 3-tuple of ints. Note `& 0xFF` **wraps** (300 -> 44, -1 -> 255) where `P.clamp8` would clamp; no shipped call site can feed it an out-of-range channel, so the difference is invisible |
| `_q8` | per channel `min(255, (int(c)+4)//8*8)` | 33: `{0, 8, 16, ..., 248, 255}` (channels >= 252 land on the `min` and give 255 - so the grid is 33 levels, not the "32-level" the docstring at 78 claims) | Snap animated colours onto a grid so a per-colour surface cache can settle. Docstring (77-83): *"Text is cached per colour, and HUD colours are animated... an exact key would mint a fresh render every single frame. A step of 8 is invisible on screen"* |
| `_step` | `round(clamp(v,0,1) * steps) / steps` | `steps+1` levels; `steps` defaults to 6 but is passed explicitly at five of the six call sites (5, 8, 4, 4, 4) | Snap a 0..1 **blend weight** so *"the shape of the animation (bar width, glow size) stays smooth while the colour settles on a handful of values"* (90-96) |

**Quantisation exists only to keep pygame's surface cache small.** Under the settled port decision
(colour is `tint`, intensity is `alpha`, and both are free) the *colour* quantisers have no job left,
while the *geometry* quantisers still do - a texture is still per-shape. Per call site:

| Call site | Line | Quantiser | Quantises | Port |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_glow_add` cache key | 153-154 | `_q8(color)` | colour | **drop** - texture is white, colour is `tint` |
| `_glow_add` cache key | 154 | `round(clamp(intensity,0,3), 1)` | intensity (31 buckets) | **drop** - intensity is `alpha`; render.md **Q1** already covers "smoother than Python is acceptable" |
| `_glow_add` cache key | 154 | `radius:int` (from `int(clamp(radius,2,260))`, 152) | **geometry** | **keep**, via `quantiseRadius` (textures.ts:213) |
| `_rrect_glow` cache key | 185-186 | `_q8(color)` | colour | **drop** |
| `_rrect_glow` cache key | 186 | `round(clamp(intensity,0,2), 1)` (21 buckets) | intensity | **drop** |
| `_rrect_glow` cache key | 186 | `w, h, int(corner), spread` | **geometry** | **keep** - and §1.9 collapses even these to one 9-sliced texture |
| `_panel_surface` key | 208 | `_q(accent)` + `alpha:int` | colour + opacity | panels section: the rim colour becomes a tint, `alpha` the sprite alpha; keep `w,h,border,corner` |
| `_text_pair` key | 255-256 | `_q8(color)` | colour | **drop** - glyph texture is white, colour is `tint` |
| `draw_bar` | 339, 341 | `_q8(color)`, `_q8(bg)`/`_q(P.UI_PANEL)` | colour | **drop** |
| everything else, i.e. every remaining `_q` | 239 (`draw_panel` accent), 364-367 + 382 (`_button_palette`), 389 (`_button_body` key), 405 (bar sheen), 516 + 582 (`Button.draw` / `_draw_icon`), 598-599 (`draw_cursor`), 735 + 753 (HUD backdrop), 830 (life icon head colour), 895 (chip colour), 933 (combo colour), 1016-1018 (`_draw_hud_impl` accent/accent2/dim), 1062 (level-name text) | `_q(...)` | colour **coercion**, not quantisation | **drop** (a `Theme` field is already an `RGB` tuple in TS) |
| `_step` | 1058, 1065, 1067, 1070, 1099, 1101 | blend weight fed straight into `P.lerp_color(...)` | **colour, all six** | **drop `_step` entirely** |

**One pygame-cache hazard the port inherits nothing of, but which explains the reference
screenshots.** `_glow_add` and `_rrect_glow` compute their falloff from the **unrounded** intensity
(165, 194) while keying on the rounded one (154, 186), so the cached surface's brightness is whatever
the *first* caller in that bucket happened to ask for - up to 0.05 of intensity away from what a
later caller wanted, forever. `Button.draw` sidesteps it by pre-rounding its own argument
(`round(power, 1)`, 522); `draw_panel` does not (it passes `clamp(glow, 0, 2)` raw, 241), so a
pulsing panel glow (menu.py:778, pause.py:353) is stepped *and* first-writer-wins. The port's smooth
`alpha` is closer to the author's intent than the shipped surface is; this is exactly render.md's
**Q1** ("smoother than Python is acceptable"), so do not try to reproduce it.

`_step` deserves the emphasis: **all six call sites feed a `lerp_color` weight and nothing else** -
the score odometer tint (1057-1058), the goal-bar colour (1065) and its white flash (1067), the goal
label (1070), the boost-bar colour (1099) and the low-stamina label pulse (1101). Nothing geometric
is ever `_step`-ed, so the function does not survive the port. Dropping it also sidesteps a real
language difference: Python's `round()` is banker's rounding (`round(2.5) == 2`) while JS
`Math.round(2.5) === 3`, so a literal port would disagree with the Python at every exact half-step
(e.g. `_step(0.625, 4)`).

### 1.5 `_rect` - tolerant rect coercion (lines 100-107)

```python
if isinstance(r, pygame.Rect): return r.copy()
try:    return pygame.Rect(int(r[0]), int(r[1]), int(r[2]), int(r[3]))   # indexable
except (TypeError, IndexError, ValueError):
        return pygame.Rect(int(r.x), int(r.y), int(r.w), int(r.h))       # .x/.y/.w/.h
```

Three branches; call sites are `draw_panel` (236), `draw_bar` (334) and `Button.__init__` (428).
What they are actually handed across the shipped game:

| Shape | Example | Branch |
|---|---|---|
| `pygame.Rect` | `gameplay.py:246` `pygame.Rect(*PAUSE_RECT)` (the pause button's `(886, 32, 70, 38)` tuple is widened to a Rect *before* it reaches `_rect`), `help_scene.py:50` `_DEMO_PANEL`, `level_select.py:75` `DETAIL_RECT`, `settings.py:91` `_BACK_RECT`, `story_scene.py:74` `_CONTINUE_RECT`, the animated READY card `gameplay.py:1355-1356` | 1 |
| int 4-tuple | `draw_panel`: `gameover.py:783` `(300, 88, 680, 480)`, `gameover.py:1080` `(272, 66, 736, 520)`. `Button.__init__`: `gameover.py:426` `(490, 620, 300, BUTTON_H)`, `gameover.py:459` `(x + i*(width+gap), y, width, BUTTON_H)` | 2 |
| `contracts.Rect` (dataclass with `x/y/w/h`, contracts.py:73-80 - **not** indexable) | none | 3 is **dead code** in the shipped game (defensive only) |

Two things the port must not copy: `_rect` **truncates floats to int** (as does `pygame.Rect`
itself), and it **copies** so the caller's rect can never be mutated. Under "float coordinates, no
integer truncation" the TS API takes a `{x,y,w,h}` (or `RectLike`, `mathx.ts:112`) of floats and
keeps them. The truncation is visible in exactly one place: the READY card's swing-in
(`gameplay.py:1356`, `panel.center = (a.centerx, a.centery - 40 + int((1.0 - intro) * 40))`) already
truncates its own animation, so nothing is lost there.

### 1.6 `_sys_font` / `_font` - the ui-module-local font fallback (lines 110-138)

This is **independent of `FontBook`** (`snake/gfx/fonts.py`) and is the reason the whole toolkit is
usable with no font book at all.

```python
def _sys_font(size, bold=False):                                    # ui.py:110
    key = (int(size), bool(bold))
    font = _FONT_CACHE.get(key)
    if font is None:
        if not pygame.font.get_init(): pygame.font.init()
        font = pygame.font.Font(None, int(size * 1.15))              # ui.py:117
        try: font.set_bold(bold)
        except Exception: pass
        _FONT_CACHE[key] = font
    return font
```

- pygame's built-in face renders visually smaller than the named UI faces at the same nominal size,
  hence the **`size * 1.15`** correction (truncated to int). The `set_bold` is best-effort.
- `_FONT_CACHE` is the module's only unbounded cache (§1.3) - bounded in practice by the ~8 sizes
  the module asks for.

```python
def _font(fonts, name, size=18, bold=False):                        # ui.py:126
    try:
        if name == "display": return fonts.display_at(size)          # sized path
        if name == "ui":      return fonts.get(size, bold)           # sized path
        got = getattr(fonts, name)                                   # duck-typed path
        if isinstance(got, pygame.font.Font): return got
    except Exception: pass
    return _sys_font(size, bold)                                     # ui.py:138
```

`getattr(fonts, name)` with **no default**, inside a bare `except Exception` - so `fonts=None`,
`fonts` missing the attribute, or an attribute holding something that is not a `pygame.font.Font`
all silently degrade to `_sys_font`. The HUD reaches its font book by
`fonts = getattr(game, "fonts", None)` (ui.py:994, in `_draw_hud_impl` - `draw_hud` itself only sets
the clip and delegates) and `Button.draw` takes `fonts` as a plain parameter (502), so **both work
with `fonts=None`** - every label just renders in the platform default face at 1.15x its nominal
size. The eight distinct `(size, bold)` pairs the module can ask the fallback for are
`(17,F) (21,F) (24,T) (26,T) (30,T) (34,F) (42,F) (14,F)`, which is the practical bound on
`_FONT_CACHE`.

**The `size`/`bold` arguments are only used by the fallback.** On the duck-typed path the FontBook's
own size wins. FontBook's faces (fonts.py:63-71): `huge=display_at(96)`, `title=display_at(64)`,
`h1=display_at(42)`, `h2=get(30, bold=True)`, `body=get(21)`, `small=get(17)`, `tiny=get(14)`,
`mono=mono_at(16)`, `mono_small=mono_at(13)`. Every call site in ui.py:

| Line | Call | Resolves to (with a FontBook) | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| 497 | `_font(fonts, "small", 17)` | `get(17)` | agrees |
| 499 | `_font(fonts, "body", 21)` | `get(21)` | agrees |
| 500 | `_font(fonts, "h2", 30, True)` | `get(30, bold)` | agrees |
| 585 | `_font(fonts, "h1", 42)` / `_font(fonts, "h2", 30, True)` | `display_at(42)` / `get(30, bold)` | agrees |
| 863 | `_font(fonts, "tiny", 14)` | `get(14)` | agrees |
| **950** | `_font(fonts, "h2", **26**, True)` | `get(**30**, bold)` | **disagrees** |
| 1044 | `_font(fonts, "tiny", 14)` | `get(14)` | agrees |
| 1052 | `_font(fonts, "display", 34)` | `display_at(34)` | sized path |
| 1062 | `_font(fonts, "ui", 24, True)` | `get(24, bold)` | sized path |

**Quirk (ported as a quirk): the combo badge asks for 26 px and gets 30 px.** ui.py:950 renders
`f"x{combo}"` with `_font(fonts, "h2", 26, True)`; because the attribute lookup succeeds, the 26 is
discarded and the badge draws at `h2` = 30 px bold. The 26 is reachable *only* with no font book. It
is visible - the badge glyphs are 15% larger than the author asked for, inside a fixed-size chip -
so **the port must render the combo count at the h2 size (30 px bold)**, not 26.

**What the port should do instead.** There is always a font in a browser, so the fallback has no
reason to exist: the fonts section should expose a strict, typed accessor (proposed
`web/src/ui/fonts.ts` -> `fonts.tiny/small/body/h2/h1/title/huge/mono/monoSmall` plus
`fonts.ui(size, bold)` and `fonts.display(size)`), and every ui call site should name the *resolved*
size from the table above. Drop `_sys_font`, drop `_FONT_CACHE`, drop the `getattr` duck-typing, and
assert in dev builds that the requested face exists (render.md §8: *"drop the fallbacks, keep
asserts"*). Note the shell dependency: `snake/main.py` gives `Game` a `fonts` attribute, whereas
`web/src/app/Game.ts` currently has **no** `fonts` field - the HUD needs one (or a module singleton)
before it can draw.

### 1.7 `_glow_add` - the ui-local radial bloom (lines 144-167)

```python
radius = int(clamp(radius, 2, 260))                                  # ui.py:152
col    = _q8(color)                                                  # ui.py:153
key    = (radius, col, round(clamp(intensity, 0.0, 3.0), 1))         # ui.py:154
size   = radius * 2                                                  # ui.py:159
surf   = pygame.Surface((size, size))          # NO SRCALPHA -> opaque black
steps  = int(clamp(radius, 5, 26))                                   # ui.py:161
for i in range(steps, 0, -1):                  # outside-in            ui.py:162
    f = i / steps                              # 1.0 at the rim, ~0 at the core
    b = ((1.0 - f) ** 2.4) * clamp(intensity, 0.0, 3.0)              # ui.py:165
    pygame.draw.circle(surf, P.shade(col, b), (radius, radius), max(1, int(radius * f)))
return _store(_GLOW_CACHE, key, surf, 512)                           # ui.py:167
```

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Sprite size | `2r x 2r`, centre `(r, r)`; `r = int(clamp(radius, 2, 260))` |
| Backing | plain `Surface` (**no `SRCALPHA`**) - zero-filled, i.e. opaque black |
| Bands | `steps = clamp(r, 5, 26)` filled circles, drawn **outside-in** so brighter inner bands *overwrite* dimmer outer ones (ui.py:149-150) |
| Band geometry | band `i` (from `steps` down to 1): radius `max(1, int(r * i/steps))` |
| Falloff | `b(f) = (1-f)**2.4 * clamp(intensity, 0, 3)` where `f = i/steps`. Comment (164): *"(1 - f) ** 2.4 mimics an optical bloom far better than a linear ramp"* |
| Band colour | `P.shade(col, b)` - falloff baked into **RGB** (palette.py:41-43, `clamp8` **truncates** per channel: palette.py:26-28, mirrored in palette.ts:30-33) |
| Peak | at `i=1`: `b = (1-1/steps)**2.4 * I`, i.e. `0.910·I` at `steps=26`, `0.585·I` at `steps=5`. **The peak depends on the band count, so it depends on the radius** - see the normalisation table below |
| Blend | consumed only via `_blit_glow` -> `BLEND_RGB_ADD` |
| Cache | `_GLOW_CACHE`, limit **512** (not 384) |
| Never raises | it can: it is only ever called from inside a caller's `try` |

Measured against the shipped module (`_glow_add` called directly under pygame 2.6.1, white at
intensity 1.0), which pins the arithmetic above:

| Call | Sprite | Centre value | Formula | Outermost non-black pixel |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_glow_add(22, white, 1.0)` (the cursor halo) | 44x44 | **228** | `trunc(255·(1-1/22)**2.4)` = `trunc(228.06)` | `f = 19/22 ≈ 0.864` |
| `_glow_add(2, white, 1.0)` | 4x4 | **149** in a 2x2 block, 0 elsewhere | `trunc(255·(1-1/5)**2.4)` = `trunc(149.25)` | - |

The `radius=2` case is the clearest illustration of the `max(1, int(radius*f))` floor: `steps` is
`clamp(2, 5, 26) = 5`, and the five band radii come out `2, 1, 1, 1, 1`, so four bands land on top of
each other and the sprite is a 2x2 square at `0.585·I` inside an all-black 4x4. Nothing about it is
soft; it is the smallest thing the module can draw and no call site asks for it.

**Is this the same primitive as render.md §1?** Effectively yes - a different exponent on the same
curve - and *not* the same as `textures.ts::radialTexture`. Writing every falloff as a function of
the radius fraction `f` (0 at the centre, 1 at the rim):

| Source | `b(f)` | f=0.25 | f=0.5 | f=0.75 | f=0.9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ui.py::_glow_add` (165) | `(1-f)**2.4` | 0.501 | 0.189 | 0.036 | 0.0040 |
| `render.py::_build_glow` (render.md §1.1) | `(1-f)²·(0.35+0.65(1-f))` | 0.471 | 0.169 | 0.032 | 0.0042 |
| `background.py::_radial` (background.py:115-142) = **`textures.ts::radialTexture`** | `1-f²` | 0.938 | 0.750 | 0.438 | 0.190 |

So `_glow_add` and render.py's `glow_surface` are the same halo to within ~6%, while the
`radialTexture` the port already ships is a **fundamentally fatter glow** (4x the brightness at half
radius). Two further differences from render.py's version, both real:

- `_glow_add` writes **no alpha channel at all** (plain surface), where `_build_glow` writes
  `alpha = 255·falloff` as well so the sprite also composites in normal mode and accumulates alpha
  under `BLEND_RGBA_ADD` (render.md §1). Nothing in ui.py needs that: there is no ghost path here.
- `_glow_add` has **no solid core disc**; `_build_glow` finishes with one of radius `max(1, r·0.12)`
  to keep tiny glows from vanishing (render.md §1.1). ui.py relies on the `max(1, ...)` band radius
  instead - the degenerate `radius=2` case measured above.
- The band counts differ: `_build_glow` uses `clamp(r·1.35, 10, 60)`, `_glow_add` uses
  `clamp(r, 5, 26)`. At the cursor's 22 px halo that is 30 bands vs 22; the banding is therefore
  slightly coarser in the UI layer than in the entity layer, which matters only in that the port
  should key its texture on the *ui* band count (§1.7 API below), not reuse render.py's.

It is also the **opposite** of the hazard glow: `obstacles.py:85-115` builds an `SRCALPHA` sprite
with the falloff in **alpha** and blits it with `BLEND_RGB_ADD`, which discards alpha - so hazard
glows ship as flat hard-edged discs (`gfx/entities/hazardGlow.ts`, and the settled decision in
memory). ui.py bakes into RGB, so **ui glows really are soft**. Do not port them as flat discs.

**Three smaller pygame artefacts, all safe to drop.**
(a) Because `clamp8` truncates per channel, the dimmest channel of a colour dies before the
brightest at the rim: for `intensity=1` a channel of 255 survives to `f≈0.90` but a channel of 96
only to `f≈0.85`, so the Python glow's hue drifts toward its dominant channel in the outermost few
percent. A white-texture-plus-tint port keeps the hue constant, because the tint multiplies after
the 8-bit texture. Perceptually invisible; do not bake for it.
(b) The outermost band is always exactly black (`i=steps` -> `f=1` -> `b=0`), so it is a no-op fill
of the whole sprite.
(c) Because the truncation happens per *band*, the cutoff lands on a band boundary rather than on the
continuous `f≈0.90`: at 22 bands the outermost surviving band is `i=19`, so the sprite is measurably
black beyond `f = 19/22 ≈ 0.864` (table above). A white texture built once at full precision and
scaled by `alpha` has no such cutoff, so the ported glow reaches its full nominal radius and is a few
percent wider than Python's at any intensity, more so at low intensity. Accept it - it is the same
class of difference as render.md **Q1**, and the alternative (baking a per-intensity cutoff) would
throw away the whole point of tint x alpha.

**TS home: extend `web/src/gfx/textures.ts`.** That module already owns the procedural texture cache
and states the "colour leaves the texture" contract in its own docstring (textures.ts:10-35). It
does **not** yet have this falloff: `drawRadialDiscs` (129-149) hard-codes
`rr = r·sqrt(1-i/n)` + `brightness = (i+1)/n`, i.e. the `1-f²` curve. Add a sibling, do not add a
second cache:

```ts
/** ui.py's `_glow_add`: white bands at b = (1-f)^UI_GLOW_EXP, brightest band normalised to 1. */
export const UI_GLOW_EXP = 2.4;                 // ui.py:165
/** Key is the BAND COUNT alone - the profile is scale-invariant in `bands`. */
export function uiGlowTexture(bands: number): Texture;
export function uiGlowBands(radius: number): number;   // clamp(trunc(clamp(r,2,260)), 5, 26)
```
with `bands = clamp(Math.trunc(clamp(radius, 2, 260)), 5, 26)` (ui.py:152 + 161). Because band `i`
sits at radius `r·i/bands`, the profile depends on `bands` and on nothing else once `r >> bands`, so
**the cache key is `bands` and the whole game needs at most 22 textures.** Do *not* put a radius in
the key (an earlier draft of this section proposed `${quantiseRadius(min(r,64))}|${bands}`, which
contradicts the scale-invariance it relies on and multiplies the cache for no gain). Build each at
`Math.min(MAX_BUILD_RADIUS, Math.max(32, bands * 4))` - four source pixels per band, capped at
textures.ts:48's 128 - and let the sprite's `width/height` carry the real radius, exactly as
`setGlow` already does (textures.ts:279-286). Keep the "bands overwrite on a scratch, the finished
glow adds" discipline (`paintRadial`, textures.ts:171-205) - adding bands directly sums the series and
comes out ~6x too bright.

Normalise the texture so the innermost band is 255 and carry the rest in `alpha`:

```
alpha = intensity · (1 - 1/bands) ** 2.4
```

**This factor is per-`bands` and must not be hoisted into a constant**, because the small dabs and the
