big blooms differ by 55%:

| bands (= radius, clamped 5..26) | 5 | 7 | 9 | 11 | 13 | 22 | 26 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| peak `(1-1/bands)**2.4` | 0.585 | 0.691 | 0.754 | 0.796 | 0.825 | 0.894 | 0.910 |

Worked through for the cursor (§6): the halo is `intensity 0.45·bright` at 22 bands ->
`alpha = 0.402` held / `0.290` idle; the core glow is `1.1·bright` at 7 bands -> `0.760` / `0.547`;
the 12 trail dabs run `alpha = 0.112 .. 0.561`. **Nothing in the cursor exceeds `alpha = 1`.**

Across the whole module only two call sites can, once normalised: the HUD combo badge (§1.8 line 938,
intensity up to 1.40 at 26 bands -> `alpha = 1.274`; the threshold is intensity `1/0.910 = 1.099`) and
the rounded-rect button halo (§1.9, power up to 1.50 x gain 0.7496 -> `alpha = 1.124`; threshold
power `1.334`). `setGlow` (textures.ts:285) clamps `alpha` to 1, so for those two, **stack a second
additive sprite carrying the remainder**: pygame clamps the source channel at 255 and the destination
add saturates at 255, and two additive sprites likewise saturate in the framebuffer, so the two are
equivalent wherever the first sprite is already at full alpha. This is the additive escape hatch from
the `shade(c, f>1)` exception - no baking needed here.

### 1.8 `_blit_glow` (lines 170-177)

```python
if intensity <= 0.01: return                                         # ui.py:173
g = _glow_add(int(radius), color, intensity)                         # ui.py:175  <- int() on radius
surface.blit(g, (int(x - g.get_width()*0.5), int(y - g.get_height()*0.5)),
             special_flags=pygame.BLEND_RGB_ADD)                     # ui.py:176-177
```

The module's `stampGlow`. Three things to note: the `intensity <= 0.01` early-out (an invisible glow
costs nothing - it never fires anywhere in the cursor, whose smallest intensity is 0.183), the
`int(radius)` truncation (so an animated radius grows in 1 px steps, and the band count with it), and
the integer blit corner. The corner is `trunc(x - g.get_width()*0.5)`; since the sprite is `2r x 2r`
with `r` already an int, that is `trunc(x - r)`, so the *centre* lands on `trunc(x)` - the glow is
displaced by up to 1 px toward the origin. Dropped in the port per the settled float-coordinates
decision.

Every `_blit_glow` call site in the module, which is also the bound on the texture cache:

| Line | Where | Radius | Intensity |
|---|---|---|---|
| 350 | bar fill head, at `(r.x + fill_w, r.centery)` | `r.h * 1.5` | `breathe` (bars section) |
| 584 | Button icon, at the icon centre | `26` | `0.25 + 0.45·hov` -> 0.25..0.70 |
| 616 | **cursor trail dab** (§6.2) | `4 + 9f` -> int **5..13** in steady state (4 occurs only on the one frame where `n = 13`, when nothing is skipped) | `0.18 + 0.5f²` -> 0.183..0.68 |
| 627 | **cursor halo** (§6.3) | `22` | `0.45·bright` -> 0.324 / 0.45 |
| 657 | **cursor core** (§6.3) | `7` | `1.1·bright` -> 0.792 / 1.1 |
| 834 | HUD life icon | `rad * 2.6` | `0.35 + 0.3·pop` |
| 903 | HUD power-up chip | `size` | `(0.30 + 0.30·pulse(t,2.4))·beat` |
| 924 | HUD chip orbit dots, at `(cx + cos(ang)·ring_r, cy + sin(ang)·ring_r)` | (per-dot) | (per-dot) |
| 938 | HUD combo badge | `26 · scale` | `0.35 + 0.55·heat + 0.5·pop` -> up to **1.40** |
| 1055 | HUD score flash, at the fixed point `(96, 46)`, colour `P.UI_GOLD` | `62` | `0.20 + 0.5·score_hit` |
| 1109 | HUD boost bar, at the fixed point `(1069, 18)` | `90` | `0.35` (constant; gated on `mouse_buttons[3]` **and** `frac > 0`) |
| 1128 | HUD under-strip bloom, at `(C.WINDOW_W*0.5, C.HUD_H - 2)` | `150` | `0.10` (constant) |

Distinct integer radii across the whole module: six fixed (7, 22, 26, 62, 90, 150), the ten dab radii
(4..13), and a handful computed from HUD/bar geometry - call it ~20 under Python's geometry-keyed
scheme. Under the port's `bands`-keyed scheme (§1.7) the same set collapses to at most **22 textures
for the entire game, shared with every other consumer of the ui falloff**. No eviction needed either
way.

**Reuse, do not re-implement, the stamping machinery.** `web/src/gfx/SnakeRenderer.ts` already has a
`SpritePool` (class at 172-202: `begin`/`next`/`end`, hiding unused sprites so a steady frame
allocates nothing and child order stays stable) and a `stampGlow` (204-215). Both are file-private;
`stampGlow(pool, x, y, radius, color: number, intensity)` takes a **pool** (not a container) and a
**packed `0xRRGGBB`** (unpacked through the shared `scratchRgb` by the exported `rgbOf`, 154-160), which
is a different signature from the `stampGlow(container, …, rgb, …)` render.md §1.4 proposed - the
promoted version should keep the pool form, since that is what ships. Promote both (e.g.
`web/src/gfx/SpritePool.ts` exporting `SpritePool`, `stampGlow`, and moving `rgbOf`/`mixHex`/`shadeHex`
to `core/palette` as their own comment at SnakeRenderer.ts:143-151 asks) and have the UI layer use
them rather than writing a third copy. `ArenaFrame.ts` is already a second consumer of `setGlow`
(imports it at 36), so the promotion pays for itself. The UI's variant needs two additions: a falloff
argument (which texture: `uiGlowTexture(bands)` vs `radialTexture`) and the `alpha > 1` two-sprite
split from §1.7.

### 1.9 `_rrect_glow` - the halo behind every panel and button (lines 180-198)

```python
w, h   = max(2, int(w)), max(2, int(h))                              # ui.py:183
spread = int(clamp(spread, 2, 34))                                   # ui.py:184
col    = _q8(color)                                                  # ui.py:185
key    = (w, h, int(corner), col, spread, round(clamp(intensity, 0.0, 2.0), 1))
surf   = pygame.Surface((w + spread*2, h + spread*2))   # plain -> opaque black   ui.py:191
for i in range(spread, 0, -1):                          # outside-in              ui.py:192
    f = i / spread
    b = ((1.0 - f) ** 2.2) * clamp(intensity, 0.0, 2.0) * 0.85       # ui.py:194
    pygame.draw.rect(surf, P.shade(col, b),
                     pygame.Rect(spread - i, spread - i, w + i*2, h + i*2),
                     border_radius=int(corner) + i)                  # ui.py:195-197
return _store(_RGLOW_CACHE, key, surf)                               # ui.py:198 (limit 384)
```

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Sprite size | `(w + 2·spread) x (h + 2·spread)`; the panel occupies the inner `w x h` at offset `(spread, spread)` |
| Rings | exactly `spread` of them, `i = spread .. 1`, drawn **outside-in** |
| Ring `i` geometry | rect `(spread-i, spread-i, w+2i, h+2i)`, **corner radius `corner + i`** |
| Ring `i` brightness | `b(i) = (1 - i/spread)**2.2 · clamp(intensity, 0, 2) · **0.85**` |
| Fill vs stroke | `pygame.draw.rect` with **no `width`** -> every ring is a **filled** rounded rect. The halo is therefore *not* a ring: the interior ends up uniformly at `b(1)` and the outer `spread` px is a ramp |
| Peak (interior) | `b(1) = (1-1/spread)**2.2 · I · 0.85`; at `spread=18` that is **`0.7496·I`** |
| Effective spread | rings with `255·b < 1` truncate to black: at `I=1, spread=18` the outer 2 rings vanish, so the visible halo is ~16 px, and a dim channel (96) dies at ~15 px. As with `_glow_add`, the ported white texture has no cutoff and reaches the full 18 px |
| Corner radius clamp | pygame clamps `border_radius` to `min(w,h)//2`; ring `i` needs `corner+i <= (h+2i)/2`, i.e. **`h >= 2·corner = 24`**. Every shipped rect satisfies it (smallest is the 70x38 pause button) |
| Exponent | 2.2 - note `_glow_add` uses 2.4 (ui.py:165 vs 194): **different curves, deliberately** |
| Blend | always `BLEND_RGB_ADD` at the call site |

**Call sites - `corner` and `spread` are constant across the shipped game.**

| Line | Caller | `w,h` | `corner` | `spread` | `intensity` | Blit position |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 241-242 | `draw_panel` | `r.w, r.h` | `C.UI_CORNER` = 12 | **18** | `clamp(glow, 0, 2)` | `(r.x - 18, r.y - 18)`, `BLEND_RGB_ADD` |
| 522-523 | `Button.draw` | `self.rect.w/h` (**unscaled**) | `C.UI_CORNER` = 12 | **18** | `round(idle + 0.85·hov + 0.5·flash, 1)`, where `idle = 0.10 + 0.05·pulse(t·1.6 + rect.x·0.01)` | `(dst.x - 18, dst.y - 18)`, `BLEND_RGB_ADD` |

Shipped `glow` values for panels: 0.20 (gameover.py:783), 0.42 (gameover.py:1080), 0.35
(help_scene.py:553, level_select.py:990), 0.25 (help_scene.py:605/624/648), `0.25 + 0.12·pulse(t,1.3)`
(menu.py:778), `0.55·vis` (gameplay.py:1358, the READY card), `0.3` (settings.py:977). Button power
peaks near **1.50** (0.15 idle + 0.85 hover + 0.5 press flash).

**Quirk (ported as a quirk): the button halo does not follow the button's hover scale.**
`Button.draw` builds the glow from the **unscaled** `w, h` (ui.py:522) but blits it at the
**scaled** `dst` corner minus 18 (523). The glow's centre therefore sits at `dst.x + w/2` while the
button's is at `dst.x + dw/2`, so at hover scale 1.035 on a 300 px button the halo is ~5 px left of
and ~1 px above the button, and stays 300x58 while the body grows to 310x60. It is visible as a
slight asymmetry on hover. The **buttons section owns the keep-or-fix call**; the default is to port
as-is (build the 9-slice sprite from `rect.w/h`, position it at `dst.topLeft - spread`).

**Port design: one 9-sliced white texture replaces the whole `_RGLOW_CACHE`.** The shape is exactly
9-sliceable, which is worth spelling out because it collapses up to 384 per-size surfaces into a
single 62x62 texture:

- Every ring's corner arc is centred at `(spread - i) + (corner + i) = spread + corner` from the
  sprite's corner - **the same point for every `i`**. So the corner region is a set of concentric
  arcs contained in a `(spread+corner)` square, and outside that square the pattern is a pure 1-D
  ramp along x (or y), with a uniform interior at `b(1)`.
- Therefore: build the texture at `w0 = h0 = 2·corner + 2` (26 px) -> texture
  `62 x 62` for `corner=12, spread=18`, and 9-slice it with all four guides =
  **`corner + spread` = 30 px**, leaving a 2x2 stretchable middle. Pixi v8:
  `new NineSliceSprite({ texture, leftWidth: 30, topHeight: 30, rightWidth: 30, bottomHeight: 30 })`,
  then `width = w + 2·spread`, `height = h + 2·spread`, `position = (x - spread, y - spread)`,
  `blendMode = "add"`, `tint = toHex(accent)`, `alpha = intensity · 0.7496`.
- Guard: a 9-slice needs the target >= the sum of its guides (60 px), i.e. `w, h >= 24` - the same
  condition as pygame's corner-radius clamp above. Every shipped rect passes; assert it in dev.
- `alpha > 1` (button power > 1.334) is handled by the same second-sprite stack as §1.7.

Suggested API, again **extending `web/src/gfx/textures.ts`** rather than adding a cache:

```ts
export const RRECT_GLOW_EXP = 2.2;    // ui.py:194
export const RRECT_GLOW_GAIN = 0.85;  // ui.py:194
/** White rounded-rect halo, 9-sliceable; key `${corner}|${spread}`. In practice one entry: 12|18. */
export function panelGlowTexture(corner: number, spread: number): { texture: Texture; slice: number };
```

### 1.10 Suggested TS homes for this section

| Python | TS home | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `_CACHE_LIMIT`, `_store`, the 6 surface caches | **nothing** | geometry-only keys make the caches self-bounding; `textures.ts` already owns the policy (textures.ts:562-587, incl. `generatedTextureStats()` for the debug overlay) |
| `_q`, `_q8`, `_step` | **nothing** | colour quantisers; `RGB` is already a tuple type in TS |
| `_rect` | `RectLike` (`mathx.ts:119` uses one) or a local `{x,y,w,h}` | keep floats |
| `_sys_font`, `_font`, `_FONT_CACHE` | fonts section (`web/src/ui/fonts.ts`) | strict accessor, no fallback; resolve sizes per §1.6's table |
| `_glow_add` | `textures.ts::uiGlowTexture(radius)` + `UI_GLOW_EXP` | new falloff; do **not** reuse `radialTexture` unverified (§1.7 table) |
| `_blit_glow` | promoted `SpritePool` + `stampGlow` from `SnakeRenderer.ts:172-215` | plus the `alpha > 1` split |
| `_rrect_glow` | `textures.ts::panelGlowTexture(corner, spread)` + `NineSliceSprite` at the call site | one texture for the whole game |

**Open question (flag, do not silently decide): which falloff do the UI glows get?**
`SnakeRenderer.ts` currently stamps *all* of render.py's glows with `radialTexture`, i.e. the
`1-f²` curve from `background.py::_radial`, not render.py's own `(1-f)²(0.35+0.65(1-f))`. Nothing in
the settled decisions covers that substitution. If it was a deliberate, screenshot-verified
simplification then the UI glows can use `radialTexture` too and `uiGlowTexture` is unnecessary; if
it was accidental, both should move to the sharper curve. The numbers in §1.7 say the difference is
large (4x at half radius), and UI glows sit under text and panel rims where a fatter halo washes out
contrast. Recommend: add `uiGlowTexture`, verify the cursor and a hovered button against
`E:/SnakeGame/captures/*.png`, and raise the render.py case separately.

---

## 2. Panels — `_panel_surface` and `draw_panel`

Ground truth: `E:/SnakeGame/snake/gfx/ui.py:201-246`. Supporting primitive `_rrect_glow`
(ui.py:180-198) is owned by §1; this section pins down only the arguments `draw_panel` hands it.

`draw_panel` is the most-used widget in the game: 15 call sites across 8 scenes, and every one of
the nine non-gameplay scenes draws at least one. It is also the *only* consumer of
`_panel_surface` besides `Button` (`_button_body`, ui.py:396, calls it with `border=False` and a
corner of 12 or 18 — see §5).

### 2.1 The size census (drives every port decision below)

Every `draw_panel(` call site, with its rect fully resolved. **All 16 rect *sizes* are static
constants**; only positions and `alpha`/`glow` animate.

| Call site | Rect source | Resolved rect | w x h | `alpha` | `glow` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gameover.py:783` | literal | `(300, 88, 680, 480)` | 680x480 | `196` | `0.20` |
| `gameover.py:1080` | literal | `(272, 66, 736, 520)` | 736x520 | `190` | `0.42` |
| `gameplay.py:1357` | `gameplay.py:1354-1356` | 720x232 centred on `(arena.centerx, arena.centery - 40 + (1-intro)*40)` | 720x232 | `int(226 * vis)` | `0.55 * vis` |
| `help_scene.py:553` | `_DEMO_PANEL`, help_scene.py:50 | `(40, 96, 496, 300)` | 496x300 | `214` | `0.35` |
| `help_scene.py:605` | `_CTRL_PANEL`, help_scene.py:52 | `(560, 96, 680, 216)` | 680x216 | `214` | `0.25` |
| `help_scene.py:624` | `_PU_PANEL`, help_scene.py:53 | `(560, 328, 680, 292)` | 680x292 | `214` | `0.25` |
| `help_scene.py:648` | `_HAZARD_PANEL`, help_scene.py:51 | `(40, 412, 496, 208)` | 496x208 | `214` | `0.25` |
| `level_select.py:990` | `DETAIL_RECT`, level_select.py:75 | `(48, 586, 1184, 98)` | **1184x98** (widest) | `224` | `0.35` |
| `menu.py:778` | `menu.py:777` | `(int(34 - (1-k)*260), 516, 286, 170)`, x slides in | 286x170 | `206` | `0.25 + 0.12*pulse(t, 1.3)` |
| `pause.py:352` | `_panel_rect`, pause.py:137-140 (`_PANEL_W=470`, `_PANEL_H=646`) | 470x646 centred, `y += int(round((1-easeOutBack(intro))*46))` | **470x646** (tallest) | `int(232 * fade)` | `0.45 + 0.25*pulse(t, 1.8)` |
| `settings.py:890` | `_ROW_DISPLAY`, settings.py:78 | `(40, 96, 800, 100)` | 800x100 | `_panel_alpha(214)` | `0.22` |
| `settings.py:898` | `_ROW_DIFF`, settings.py:79 | `(40, 204, 800, 172)` | 800x172 | `_panel_alpha(214)` | `0.22` |
| `settings.py:929` | `_ROW_SOUND`, settings.py:80 | `(40, 384, 800, 80)` | **800x80** (shortest) | `_panel_alpha(214)` | `0.22` |
| `settings.py:943` | `_ROW_FX`, settings.py:81 | `(40, 472, 800, 112)` | 800x112 | `_panel_alpha(214)` | `0.22` |
| `settings.py:949` / `:956` | `_ROW_RESET`, settings.py:82 | `(40, 592, 800, 96)` | 800x96 | `_panel_alpha(214)` | `0.35 + 0.25*pulse(t, 4.0)` while confirming, else `0.22` |
| `settings.py:977` | `_PREVIEW_PANEL`, settings.py:84 (`_PREVIEW_X=872`, `_PREVIEW_W=1280-872-40=368`) | `(872, 96, 368, 524)` | 368x524 | `_panel_alpha(216)` | `0.30` |

`_panel_alpha(full)` (settings.py:865-874) = `int(clamp(full * (0.45 + 0.55 * easeOutCubic(clamp(intro, 0, 1))), 0, 255))`.

Counts that matter:

- **16 distinct sizes, 16 distinct heights, 9 distinct widths** (680 x3, 800 x5, 496 x2, and
  736 / 720 / 1184 / 286 / 470 / 368 once each).
- **`corner` is always `C.UI_CORNER = 12`** for panels. Nothing but `Button` ever passes another
  value (ui.py:395: `C.UI_CORNER + (6 if style == "tile" else 0)`).
- **4 of 16 animate `alpha`** (gameplay READY, pause, and the two settings paths).
  **7 of 16 animate `glow`.** No call site animates size.
- Nobody passes `border=False`. Nobody passes a `glow` above `0.70`.
- `settings.py`'s `danger=True` (settings.py:876-885) changes only the *caption colour*; the panel
  rim is `theme.accent` in every single case. Ported as a quirk if you were tempted to make the
  reset row's panel red — it is not.

Full-size baking of those 16 sizes costs `2,582,672 px x 4 B = 9.85 MiB` of texture. Keep that
number in mind for §2.7.

### 2.2 `_panel_surface(w, h, alpha, border, accent, corner)` — layer order (ui.py:204-229)

Positional signature, no defaults (every argument is supplied by the caller):

```python
_panel_surface(w: int, h: int, alpha: int, border: bool, accent: RGB, corner: int) -> Surface
```

`w, h = max(2, int(w)), max(2, int(h))` (ui.py:207). Surface is `pygame.Surface((w, h), SRCALPHA)`.

Layers, in composition order:

| # | Layer | Exact construction | Line |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Vertical gradient fill** | for `y` in `0..h-1`: `f = (y / max(1, h-1)) ** 0.75`; `c = lerp_color(UI_PANEL_LIGHT, UI_PANEL, f)`; `draw.line(surf, with_alpha(c, alpha), (0, y), (w, y))` | 214-217 |
| 2 | **Rounded corner mask** | `mask = Surface((w,h), SRCALPHA)`; `draw.rect(mask, (255,255,255,255), (0,0,w,h), border_radius=corner)`; `surf.blit(mask, (0,0), special_flags=BLEND_RGBA_MULT)` | 220-222 |
| 3 | **Neon rim** (`if border`) | `draw.rect(surf, with_alpha(accent, 120), (0,0,w,h), 2, border_radius=corner)` — **width 2, strokes inward** | 225 |
| 4 | **Inner top highlight** (`if border`) | `draw.line(surf, with_alpha(lerp_color(accent, UI_WHITE, 0.55), 90), (corner, 1), (w - corner, 1))` — width 1 (default), single row at `y = 1`, `x` from `corner` to `w - corner` **inclusive** | 227-228 |

There is **no** drop shadow, no outer stroke, no second gradient, and no inner fill inset. Four
layers, that is all.

The gradient is **theme-independent** — `P.UI_PANEL_LIGHT` and `P.UI_PANEL` are module constants
(`palette.ts` already exports `UI_PANEL_LIGHT = [28, 33, 56]` and `UI_PANEL = [16, 19, 34]`, from
`themes.json.ui`). Only the rim and the highlight depend on the theme. That split is the whole
basis of the port in §2.7.

Sample gradient values (exact, `lerp_color` truncates per channel):

| `y/(h-1)` | `f = t**0.75` | `lerp_color(UI_PANEL_LIGHT, UI_PANEL, f)` |
|---|---|---|
| 0.00 | 0.0000 | `(28, 33, 56)` |
| 0.25 | 0.3536 | `(23, 28, 48)` |
| 0.50 | 0.5946 | `(20, 24, 42)` |
| 0.75 | 0.8059 | `(18, 21, 38)` |
| 1.00 | 1.0000 | `(16, 19, 34)` |

The `0.75` exponent front-loads the darkening: the light band is confined to the top ~10 % of the
panel. Do not substitute a linear ramp, and do not use a CSS `linear-gradient` (the browser
interpolates linearly in sRGB and will not apply the exponent) — build it row by row.

Rim alpha is **120**, highlight alpha is **90**, and *neither scales with the `alpha` argument*.
See §2.6 hazard H1.

The highlight at `y = 1` lands **inside** the 2-px rim (which occupies `y = 0..1`), so it
overwrites the rim's second row along the top edge. The finished top edge of a bordered panel is
therefore: `y=0` → `accent @ 120`, `y=1` → `lerp(accent, UI_WHITE, 0.55) @ 90`, for
`x ∈ [corner, w-corner]`; outside that x range `y=1` stays `accent @ 120`.

### 2.3 The panel cache and its key (ui.py:56, 63-68, 208-209, 229)

```python
key = (w, h, int(alpha), bool(border), accent, int(corner))     # ui.py:208
```

| Key component | Quantisation | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `w`, `h` | `max(2, int(...))` | truncated to int by the caller's `_rect()` first |
| `alpha` | **`int()` only — not bucketed** | this is the hazard, H3 |
| `border` | `bool` | |
| `accent` | `_q(theme.accent)` (ui.py:239) — plain 8-bit ints, **not** `_q8` | so the key holds the exact accent: one entry per theme, no 8-level snapping |
| `corner` | `int()` | always 12 for panels |

`_PANEL_CACHE` uses the default `_CACHE_LIMIT = 384` and `_store` **clears the whole cache** when
it fills (ui.py:63-68) — not an LRU, not FIFO: a wholesale wipe.

### 2.4 `draw_panel` — full signature and blit order (ui.py:232-246)

```python
def draw_panel(surface, rect, theme, *, alpha: int = 210, border: bool = True,
               glow: float = 0.0) -> None
```

Body, in order:

1. `r = _rect(rect)` (ui.py:100-107) — accepts a `pygame.Rect`, a `contracts.Rect` or a 4-tuple,
   and **truncates every component to `int`**.
2. Guard: `if r.w < 2 or r.h < 2: return`.
3. `accent = _q(getattr(theme, "accent", P.UI_WHITE))`. `theme.accent` is the only theme field
   `draw_panel` reads. Drop the `getattr` fallback in TS (`Theme.accent` is non-optional).
4. **Glow pass**, `if glow > 0.01`:
   ```python
   g = _rrect_glow(r.w, r.h, C.UI_CORNER, accent, 18, clamp(glow, 0.0, 2.0))
   surface.blit(g, (r.x - 18, r.y - 18), special_flags=pygame.BLEND_RGB_ADD)
   ```
   Spread is the literal `18`, hard-coded at both the build and the blit. The halo surface is
   `(w + 36) x (h + 36)`, so the panel's additive footprint is the rect **inflated by 18 px on all
   four sides**.
5. **Body blit**, always: `surface.blit(_panel_surface(r.w, r.h, int(clamp(alpha, 0, 255)),
   border, accent, C.UI_CORNER), r.topleft)` — normal (source-over) blend, per-pixel alpha.
6. The whole function is wrapped in `try: ... except Exception: pass`. It never raises.

**The halo is under the body.** Because the body is translucent (typical `alpha` 190-232 of 255),
the halo shows *through* the card. This is load-bearing: see H7.

What `_rrect_glow` does with those arguments (ui.py:180-198 — §1 owns the primitive, these are the
facts `draw_panel` depends on):

- `spread = int(clamp(18, 2, 34)) = 18`; colour key is `_q8(accent)` (8-level snap, unlike the
  body's exact `_q`); intensity key is `round(clamp(glow, 0.0, 2.0), 1)` — **snapped to 0.1**.
- Bands `i = spread..1`, each an *opaque, overwriting* filled rounded rect
  `Rect(spread-i, spread-i, w + 2i, h + 2i)` with `border_radius = 12 + i` and colour
  `shade(accent, b)` where `b = ((1 - i/spread) ** 2.2) * clamp(intensity, 0, 2) * 0.85`.
- **There is no `i = 0` band.** The innermost band (`i = 1`) is a filled rounded rect covering the
  panel footprint at `b = (17/18)**2.2 * 0.85 * intensity = 0.7597 * intensity`. So the halo is a
  *lit slab* with an 18-px feather, not a rim glow (H7).
- Brightness is **linear in intensity** → expressible as sprite `alpha` over a baked white halo.
- Built on a plain (non-`SRCALPHA`) surface → opaque black backing, correct for `BLEND_RGB_ADD`.

### 2.5 The semantics of `alpha`, `border`, `glow` — pinned down

`integration.md` §9 quotes `draw_panel(alpha = 226*vis, border = true, glow = 0.55*vis)`. Exactly
what those mean:

| Argument | Type / range | Default | Applies to | Clamp | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `alpha` | **int, 0-255** | `210` | the **gradient body only** (layer 1) | `int(clamp(alpha, 0, 255))` at ui.py:243, then `clamp8` inside `with_alpha` | a uniform per-pixel alpha across the whole body; the rim (120) and highlight (90) are **unaffected** |
| `border` | bool | `True` | layers 3 and 4 together | — | no call site passes `False`; `Button` does |
| `glow` | **float, 0..2, scalar on *intensity only*** | `0.0` | the additive halo | `> 0.01` gate, then `clamp(glow, 0, 2)`, then `round(..., 1)` inside the cache key | **never affects radius**: spread is the constant 18, corner the constant 12. `1.0` is not a ceiling — but no panel call site exceeds `0.70`, so a port can map `glow → sprite.alpha` directly |

So: `alpha = 226*vis` fades the frosted body from 226/255 down to 0 while the neon rim stays at
full strength; `glow = 0.55*vis` dims the halo without shrinking it.

### 2.6 pygame-vs-Pixi hazards

**H1 — the rim does not fade with the body.** `alpha` is applied only to layer 1. At
`vis = 0.05` the READY card is a full-strength `accent @ 120` rounded outline around almost
nothing. **Ported as a quirk** — visible on every fade-in/out (READY card, pause intro, settings
intro). The natural Pixi port (one sprite, `sprite.alpha = alpha/255`) would fade the rim too and
must not be used for the whole panel; keep the rim on its own sprite at a fixed `alpha = 120/255`.

**H2 — `pygame.draw.*` writes RGBA; it does not blend** (module docstring, ui.py:10-13). Layers 3
and 4 land *after* the mask multiply and **replace** the body pixels underneath. Consequences:

- The rim is `accent` at `120/255` composited over **whatever is behind the panel**, not over the
  panel fill.
- The highlight row is `lerp(accent, UI_WHITE, 0.55)` at `90/255` over the background, with no rim
  underneath it.

In Pixi a stroke composites over the fill, which makes the rim read heavier and more opaque than
Python's. The house solution already exists: `web/src/gfx/entities/hazardSlab.ts` handles the
identical "pygame replaces alpha" problem by writing `ImageData` directly. For panels the cheaper
route is a **hole**: bake the body texture with the 2-px rim band cleared (`destination-out`
stroke, then `source-over` nothing), so the rim sprite on top is the only thing in that band. Do
the same for the highlight row inside the rim texture's middle slices (see §2.7). Cache key gains
`border` (Python's already has it).

**H3 — `alpha` is in the cache key, unbucketed.** The four fading call sites mint a brand-new
`w x h` surface **every frame** (up to 255 distinct alphas x 16 sizes x 12 themes), repeatedly
tripping `_CACHE_LIMIT = 384` and wiping `_PANEL_CACHE` wholesale — which also throws away the
button bodies that share it. The port fixes this for free: `alpha` becomes `sprite.alpha` and the
texture cache keys on geometry only. **Do not** replicate the alpha bucketing; there is nothing to
replicate.

**H4 — glow intensity is snapped to 0.1 in the cache key** (ui.py:186). `menu.py`'s
`0.25 + 0.12*pulse(t, 1.3)` therefore steps among `0.2 / 0.3 / 0.4` rather than sweeping its true
0.25..0.37 range (note `round(0.25, 1) == 0.2` in Python — 0.25 is exact in binary, so banker's
rounding goes down). The port's
continuous `sprite.alpha` is *smoother than Python*. Same call as `render.md` **Q1**; stay
consistent with whatever that resolves to (recommend: accept smoother, perceptual verification).

**H5 — `BLEND_RGBA_MULT` mask.** The mask is only ever `(255,255,255,255)` or `(0,0,0,0)`, so the
multiply is exactly canvas `globalCompositeOperation = "destination-in"` with a white
`roundRect` fill. Use that.

**H6 — `BLEND_RGB_ADD` ignores source alpha**, so the halo bakes its falloff into RGB on a black
surface. Follow the settled convention: compose the 18 bands **source-over** into a scratch canvas
(they overwrite; adding them sums the series and comes out several times too bright), then add the
finished halo once via a single `blendMode: "add"` sprite. This is the same rule
`textures.ts::paintRadial` documents.

**H7 — the halo has a bright plateau under the whole card**, not a rim glow (no `i = 0` band).
Because the body is translucent, that plateau lifts the card's interior. A port that
"optimises" the halo into a ring will lose the lift on every panel in the game. Verify against
`captures/09-pause.png` (glow 0.45-0.70, the strongest in the game).

**H8 — pygame's `border_radius` corners are not anti-aliased**; canvas `roundRect` is. Panel
corners will be visibly smoother in the port. Accept it (perceptual verification, not pixel-diff);
listed as **Q2** below.

**H9 — integer truncation.** `_rect()` truncates the rect, and `menu.py`'s slide-in x is already
`int(...)`. Per the settled decision, keep floats in the port; the residual difference is <= 1 px on
two sliding panels.

### 2.7 TS home and shape

**New module `web/src/gfx/ui/panel.ts`.** It must import its canvas plumbing from the existing
`web/src/gfx/textures.ts` (`createCanvas`, `context2d`, `canvasTexture`, `cssRgb`,
`whiteTexture`) — **do not add a second canvas/upload helper or a second texture cache module.**
`hazardSlab.ts` is the precedent: a per-subsystem texture cache with its own
`clear…Cache()` / `…CacheSize()` pair, plumbing borrowed from `textures.ts`.

**Recommendation: `NineSliceSprite` for all three layers, plus one `whiteTexture()` sprite for the
highlight.** Justification below; the size census in §2.1 is what settles it.

```ts
// web/src/gfx/ui/panel.ts
export const PANEL_ALPHA_DEFAULT = 210;      // ui.py:233
export const PANEL_RIM_ALPHA     = 120;      // ui.py:225
export const PANEL_RIM_WIDTH     = 2;        // ui.py:225
export const PANEL_HILITE_ALPHA  = 90;       // ui.py:227
export const PANEL_HILITE_MIX    = 0.55;     // ui.py:227
export const PANEL_GRADIENT_GAMMA = 0.75;    // ui.py:215
export const PANEL_GLOW_SPREAD   = 18;       // ui.py:241
export const PANEL_GLOW_MAX      = 2.0;      // ui.py:241
export const PANEL_GLOW_BANDS_GAMMA = 2.2;   // ui.py:194
export const PANEL_GLOW_SCALE    = 0.85;     // ui.py:194

/** 26 x h: vertical gradient + rounded mask, alpha 255, rim band punched out if `border`. */
export function panelBodyTexture(h: number, corner?: number, border?: boolean): Texture;
/** 26 x 26: white rounded-rect stroke, width 2; the highlight row cleared in the middle slices. */
export function panelRimTexture(corner?: number, width?: number): Texture;
/** 62 x 62: the 18-band white halo, bands composed source-over. */
export function panelGlowTexture(corner?: number, spread?: number): Texture;

export class Panel extends Container {
  setRect(x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number): void;
  /** `alpha255` is Python's 0-255 `alpha`; `glow` is Python's 0-2 intensity. */
  setStyle(accent: RGB, alpha255: number, border: boolean, glow: number): void;
}
export function clearPanelTextureCache(): void;
export function panelTextureCacheSizes(): { body: number; rim: number; glow: number };
```

Child order inside `Panel` (= z-order), mirroring ui.py's blit order:

```
[0] halo  NineSliceSprite  blendMode "add",  tint accent, alpha = clamp(glow,0,2)
[1] body  NineSliceSprite  normal,           tint 0xffffff (untinted), alpha = alpha255/255
[2] rim   NineSliceSprite  normal,           tint accent, alpha = 120/255      (visible iff border)
[3] hi    Sprite(whiteTexture())             tint lerp(accent, UI_WHITE, 0.55), alpha = 90/255
```

Keep `container.alpha === 1` always — putting the fade on the container would scale the additive
halo and the rim as well, which is exactly the quirk H1 says must *not* happen.

Slice geometry:

| Layer | Texture | Borders (L/R/T/B) | Sprite size / position | Cache key |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| halo | `(2*(corner+spread)+2)² = 62x62` white | `30 / 30 / 30 / 30` | `(w+36) x (h+36)` at `(x-18, y-18)` | `(corner, spread)` → **1 texture** |
| body | `(2*corner+2) x h = 26 x h` | `12 / 12 / 1 / 1` | `w x h` at `(x, y)` | `(h, corner, border)` → **16 textures** |
| rim | `(2*corner+2)² = 26x26` white stroke | `12 / 12 / 12 / 12` | `w x h` at `(x, y)` | `(corner, width)` → **1 texture** (2 with Button's corner 18) |
| highlight | `whiteTexture()` (1x1) | n/a | `(w - 2*corner + 1) x 1` at `(x + corner, y + 1)` | none |

Why 9-slice is exact here, not an approximation:

- **The halo is a perfect 9-slice.** Away from the corners, band `i` covers row `y >= spread - i`,
  so brightness at row `y < spread` is `((y/spread) ** 2.2) * 0.85` — a function of `y` only,
  uniform in `x`. Same by symmetry on the left/right edges. The corner cells are a fixed 30x30, and
  the centre cell is the `i = 1` plateau, uniform. Every slice stretches without error.
  Constraint: needs `w + 36 >= 60` and `h + 36 >= 60`, i.e. `w, h >= 24`. Smallest panel is
  `800x80`; smallest Button is comfortably above 24 too.
- **The rim is a perfect 9-slice** for the same reason (a rounded-rect stroke is the canonical
  9-slice shape). Bonus: the highlight row's hole belongs in the *middle-top* slice only, and the
  hairline starts at exactly `x = corner` — the slice boundary. Clear row `y = 1` of the texture for
  `x ∈ [12, 14)` and the port reproduces H2's replace semantics on the top edge exactly.
- **The body is 3-sliceable horizontally**, not vertically: the gradient varies per row. That is
  fine, because the texture height *is* the panel height, so the vertical scale factor is 1 and
  every slice — including `topHeight = bottomHeight = 1` — is drawn 1:1. Use `1` rather than `0`
  for the top/bottom borders: it sidesteps any question about how Pixi's `NineSliceGeometry`
  handles a zero-height row, and with `texture.height === sprite.height` the result is identical
  either way. Columns `x >= corner` of a rounded rect are fully opaque for all `y`, so the middle
  column stretches cleanly.

Why not the alternatives:

- **Per-instance `Graphics`.** Would need a per-frame rebuild for the 4 panels whose `alpha`
  animates (Graphics has no cheap alpha-only path for a *baked* gradient), cannot express the
  per-row `f ** 0.75` gradient without 80-646 `rect` calls per panel per frame, and cannot express
  H2's alpha-replacing rim at all. Reject.
- **One fully baked `w x h` texture per panel.** Simple and exact, but 9.85 MiB for the 16 sizes
  (§2.1) versus **431 KiB** for the 16 body strips + 2 shared textures — a 23x saving, and it
  matters for the planned Capacitor build. Also, baking full-size buys nothing: every size uses the
  same corner and the same gradient law.
- **A tinted single sprite for the whole panel.** Impossible: the gradient is a two-colour ramp
  between `UI_PANEL_LIGHT` and `UI_PANEL` (not a shade of one colour) and the rim needs a different
  alpha from the body. Splitting into 3 sprites is what makes both `alpha` and `accent` free.

Retained-mode fit: because all 16 sizes are static, every scene builds its `Panel` objects once in
`enter()` and per frame touches only `position`, `sprite.alpha` and `tint`. Zero allocations, four
draw objects per panel.

Constants provenance: **`UI_CORNER = 12` already exists** (`web/src/core/config.ts:142`, from
`config.json` key `"UI_CORNER"`). `UI_PANEL`, `UI_PANEL_LIGHT`, `UI_WHITE` already exist in
`web/src/core/palette.ts:281-287`. Everything else in this section is a literal inside `ui.py` that
`snake/config.py` does not export, so `tools/export_data.py` cannot supply it — the port **must add**
those numbers as named exports in `web/src/gfx/ui/panel.ts` (listed above), each carrying its
`ui.py:LINE` reference. Do not silently inline them.

Verification captures: `captures/09-pause.png` (470x646, strongest glow), `captures/04-settings.png`
(five 800-wide rows + the 368x524 preview), `captures/07-help.png` (four panels, two widths),
`captures/03-level-select.png` (the 1184x98 detail strip), `captures/10-gameover.png` and
`captures/11-victory.png` (680x480, 736x520), `captures/08-gameplay-ready.png` (720x232 READY card).
