   exact ties reachable in normal play. Implementation in 7.5.7.
4. ~~pygame's thick-line row coverage for the bottom rule.~~ Verified twice on
   pygame 2.6.1 / SDL 2.28.4 (shipped surface + isolated probe): width-2 at `y=76` covers rows
   76-77, growing downward; width-3 straddles. Re-check only if the reference pygame changes —
   nothing else here depends on the line rasteriser.
5. ~~No width clamp on `_draw_odometer`.~~ Left unclamped deliberately; the exact break point is
   now written down (7.5.4): grouped values up to `999,999,999` fit, `1,000,000,000` crosses the
   `x = 256` separator.
## 8. HUD widgets: life icons, effect icons, effect chips, combo

Ground truth: `snake/gfx/ui.py:821-959` (five private helpers), placed by
`_draw_hud_impl` (`ui.py:1072-1125`). Every helper has **exactly one call site**; there is no other
consumer anywhere in `snake/` (verified by grep), so their signatures are free to change in the port.

### 8.0 Scope, clocks, and the numbers each widget is handed

| Helper | Lines | Call site | Cadence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `_draw_life_icon` | 821-838 | `ui.py:1085`, once per icon (**3-5** icons, never fewer - see below) | per frame |
| `_draw_effect_icon` | 841-878 | `ui.py:906` only (inside the chip) | per frame, per chip |
| `_chip_bg` | 881-889 | `ui.py:904` | cached surface |
| `_draw_effect_chip` | 892-925 | `ui.py:1122`, once per active effect (0-6; the list is truncated `[:6]` at `ui.py:1114` and there are only six kinds, so the slice never bites) | per frame |
| `_draw_combo` | 928-957 | `ui.py:1092`, only when `combo > 1` | per frame |

**The clock.** `draw_hud` is called with `t = float(game.time)` (`scenes/gameplay.py:1216`), the
**raw app clock** (`main.py:402`, `self.time += dt`) - *not* `clock_t` and *not* `hazard_t`. So no HUD
widget animation is affected by slow-mo, `fx.time_scale()` or the hazard multiplier, and all of them
keep running while the pause overlay is up (`Game.update` still ticks `self.time`). The TS `Game`
already exposes exactly this field (`web/src/app/Game.ts:74`, `:232`) - use `game.time`, and do not
pass a scene clock.

**The HUD's own dt.** `dt = clamp(t - anim.last_t, 0.0, 0.1)` (`ui.py:996-997`). All pop decays below
use this dt, so a frame longer than 100 ms decays the pops as if it were 100 ms.

**One widget here mixes clocks.** The *number* the chip is handed, `remaining`, comes from
`ActiveEffects` which is ticked with the **scaled** sim dt - `self.effects.update(sdt)`
(`gameplay.py:633`, `sdt = clamp(dt * fx.time_scale(), 0, MAX_DT)`) - while the chip's own halo
breathe, its urgency strobe and its whitening all run on the unscaled `t`. So under the `slow`
power-up (or any `fx.time_scale()` dip) the ring depletes in slow motion *while the strobe keeps
wall-clock rate*, and the `remaining <= 2.0` urgency threshold arrives late in real time. Same split
as `integration.md` §10 describes for popups vs sim: the port must feed `EffectChipRow.update` the
sim's `effects.items()` and the shell's `game.time`, never one clock for both. Effects also do not
tick at all during READY (`integration.md` §9 / invariant 5), so the chips freeze with their halos
still breathing.

**Layout constants** (all literals in `ui.py`, none of them in `config.py`, therefore **none of them
in `web/src/data/config.json`** - see §8.6):

| What | Value | Line |
|---|---|---|
| `LIVES` caption | `tiny`, `theme.text_dim`, top-left `(636, 5)` | 1073 |
| life icon centres | `(654 + i * pitch, 44)`; `pitch = min(34.0, span / max(1, icons))`, `span = 140.0` | 1080-1085 |
| icon count | `icons = min(slots, max_icons)`, `max_icons = 6`, `slots = max(START_LIVES, shown_lives)`, `shown_lives = max(lives, 0)` | 1074-1081 |
| overflow text | `"+{slots-6}"`, `tiny`, dim, at `(658 + icons*pitch, 37)` | 1086-1088 |
| combo badge centre | `(812, 40)` | 1092 |
| `COMBO` caption (combo <= 1) | `tiny`, `shade(dim, 0.7)`, **centre**-aligned `(812, 33)`, **with** the default drop shadow | 1094 |
| chip centres | `y = 50`; `x = (1280-20) - 38*n + 38*0.5 + i*38` = `1279 - 38*n + 38*i` | 1118-1122 |
| chip fallback text | `"NO ACTIVE POWER-UPS"`, `tiny`, `shade(dim,0.6)`, right-aligned `(1260, 45)` | 1124-1125 |

`lives` starts at `lives_for(diff)` = 5/3/2/1 (`difficulty.py:216/238/260/282` via
`difficulty.py:430-437`, floored at 1), is re-armed per level at `gameplay.py:468` and only ever
decremented inside a run (`gameplay.py:991`, floored at 0 by `:1013-1014`). So
**`slots` is only ever 3, 4 or 5** (not 3 or 5: at 5 lives, losing one gives `slots = max(3, 4) = 4`)
and **`pitch` is only ever 34.0 (icons 3 or 4) or 28.0 (icons 5)**. The `max_icons` / `+n` overflow
path at `ui.py:1086-1088` is therefore **unreachable in the shipped game**. Port it anyway (it is
4 lines) but do not spend layout effort on it: at 5 lives the icon row's right edge is
`766 + 8.4 + 5.2 = 779.6`, clear of the badge, and the `+n` text would have landed at x=798, *inside*
the popped badge - the code comment at `ui.py:1076-1077` is only true because the branch never runs.

**The life row visibly re-spaces itself.** Because `pitch` is derived from `icons` and `icons` from
`slots = max(3, lives)`, the *first* death on a 5-life difficulty drops the row from 5 icons at
pitch 28 to 4 icons at pitch 34, i.e. the centres jump `654/682/710/738/766` ->
`654/688/722/756`. The row is left-anchored at 654, so every icon but the first slides right by up to
18 px on a single frame. Ships, and it is the most visible thing about losing a life on Easy - see
§8.9.12.

Every **widget centre** in this section is integral in the shipped game (`654 + i*34` or `654 + i*28`,
`44`; `812, 40`; `1279 - 38n + 38i`, `50`), so the `int(cx), int(cy)` in `_draw_effect_icon`
(`ui.py:844`) and the `int(cx - 13)` chip blit (`ui.py:905`) are **no-ops**, not truncations. The real
truncations are all *inside* the widgets: the life discs' bobbing centres, the ring-dot and pip
positions, the glow radii and the badge radius (§8.7).

Chips and the badge stamp additive glows of `int`-truncated radius 26 and up to `int(26*1.495) = 38`;
`draw_hud` clips everything to `(0, 0, 1280, 78)` (`ui.py:975-989`). Worth pinning, because the
author's first instinct is wrong: **nothing in this section is actually clipped.** The largest stamp,
the combo halo at peak pop, is a 76x76 sprite blitted at `(774, 2)` and so occupies rows 2..77 of a
0..77 strip - it grazes the edge and fits. The port's HUD container still needs the rectangular mask,
but for §7's glows (score bloom r=62 at y=46, boost r=90 at y=18, under-strip bloom r=150 at y=76),
not for these widgets. With six chips the row spans x 1032..1260 and its halos reach up to y=24,
exactly the bottom edge of the boost bar `Rect(958, 12, 222, 12)` (`ui.py:1103`) - they graze, they
do not overlap.

**Animation state consumed here** lives on `_HudAnim` (`ui.py:693-710`), one per `Game` via a
`WeakKeyDictionary` (`ui.py:713-729`). §7.2 owns the full 11-field class and the driver block; this
section needs four fields, whose update rules are `ui.py:1029-1040`:

| Field | Set to 1.0 when | Decay | Init |
|---|---|---|---|
| `combo_pop` | `combo > prev_combo` | `-= dt * 3.0`, floored at 0 -> 0.3333 s | 0.0 |
| `prev_combo` | `= combo` every frame | - | 0 |
| `life_pop` | `prev_lives >= 0 and lives != prev_lives` | `-= dt * 2.4`, floored at 0 -> 0.4167 s | 0.0 |
| `prev_lives` | `= lives` every frame | - | **-1** |

The `prev_lives >= 0` guard is load-bearing, but it only suppresses the **first HUD frame of the
`Game`'s life** - it is not a per-level guard. `_HudAnim` is keyed on the *Game*, so `prev_lives`
survives level and scene changes, while `gameplay.py:468` re-arms `lives` on every level start. A
level entered after any death therefore sees `lives != prev_lives` and sets `life_pop = 1.0` on
frame 1. §7.8 already registers that as a shipped quirk ("one life icon pops on frame 1"); it is in
fact invisible here because the popped index is not drawn (quirk 2 below). **Do not reset the anim in
`onEnter`** - initialise `prevLives = -1` once, when the `Hud` is constructed, exactly as
`_HudAnim.__init__` does (`ui.py:708`). §7.2.4 makes the same call.

### 8.1 `_draw_life_icon(surface, cx, cy, theme, alive, t, phase, pop)` - `ui.py:821-838`

A four-segment mini snake facing **right**, drawn head-first (i=0 is the head, and it is the
rightmost disc). `s = 1.0 + 0.35 * pop` scales positions, bob amplitude and radii together
(`ui.py:824`). No argument has a default; all eight are passed positionally from the one call site.

**Draw order inside one icon matters.** The loop runs i = 0 -> 3, so each segment is painted *over*
the one in front of it: the head disc (radius 5.2 at +8.4) is 5.6 px from segment 1 (radius 4.4 at
+2.8) and their discs overlap by 4 px, which segment 1 then overwrites. The head therefore sits at the
**bottom** of the z-order and reads as a crescent, not a full circle, and its `snake_head` colour is
clipped on its left by the darker `body_at(1/3)`. The port's sprite order must match (head added
first); the eye sprite is added last, on top of everything.

Per segment `i in 0..3` (`ui.py:825-835`):

| i | `f = i/3` | `px - cx` = `(1.5-i)*5.6*s` | `rad` = `(5.2-2.4f)*s` | `int(rad)` at s=1 | colour |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.0000 | `+8.4 s` | `5.2 s` | 5 | `_q(theme.snake_head)` |
| 1 | 0.3333 | `+2.8 s` | `4.4 s` | 4 | `theme.body_at(1/3)` |
| 2 | 0.6667 | `-2.8 s` | `3.6 s` | 3 | `theme.body_at(2/3)` |
| 3 | 1.0000 | `-8.4 s` | `2.8 s` | 2 | `theme.body_at(1.0)` = `snake_b` |

`theme.body_at(0)` (= `snake_a`) is **never used**: i=0 takes `snake_head` instead. The TS equivalent
is `bodyAt(theme, f)` (`web/src/core/palette.ts:175`). The disc radius is `max(1, int(rad))`
(`ui.py:835`); the `max(1, ...)` never binds, because the smallest `int(rad)` is 2.

- **Vertical bob** (`ui.py:828`): `py = cy + sin(t*3.4 + phase - i*0.8) * 2.2 * s`. The caller passes
  `phase = iconIndex * 1.7` (`ui.py:1085`), so for icon k, segment i the angle is
  `t*3.4 + 1.7k - 0.8i`: 0.541 Hz, each icon offset 1.7 rad from its neighbour, each segment lagging
  0.8 rad behind the one in front. Amplitude 2.2 px (2.97 px at peak pop). This is the one place in the
  icon where Python truncates a *position* (`int(px), int(py)`, `ui.py:835`), so the Python bob is a
  1 px staircase; the port's is smooth, per the settled float-coordinate decision.
- **Head glow** (`ui.py:834`), alive icons only, i=0 only: `_blit_glow(px, py, rad*2.6, col, 0.35 + 0.3*pop)`
  -> radius `13.52 * s`, which `_blit_glow` truncates to `int` -> a **13 px** glow texture at s = 1
  (`ui.py:175`), intensity **always exactly 0.35** (see the quirk below). Under §1.7's normalisation
  the sprite alpha to write is `0.35 * (1 - 1/13)**2.4 = 0.2889` (13 bands at r = 13).
- **Spent (dead) icon** (`ui.py:831-832`): `col = shade(lerp_color(col, UI_DIM, 0.8), 0.30)` applied to
  *every* segment including the head, **and no head glow and no eye dot**. Both factors are < 1, so
  this is expressible as a tint - no baking needed. Worked example, Neon Grid head
  `(180,255,255)`: `lerp_color(.., UI_DIM, 0.8) -> (141,167,191)` (Python's `clamp8` truncates),
  `shade(.., 0.30) -> (42,50,57)`.
- **Eye dot** (`ui.py:836-838`), alive only: filled circle, colour `UI_PANEL` (dark, i.e. a *hole*),
  radius **1**, centre `(cx + 6.4*s, cy - 1)`.

**Quirks, ported as quirks:**

1. **The eye does not follow the head.** The eye is placed from `cx, cy` while the head disc is at
   `cx + 8.4s, cy + sin(...)*2.2s`. The head bobs +/-2.2 px under a static eye, so the eye visibly
   slides around inside the head (and at the extremes sits on its rim: the offset is -2.0*s in x, and
   the head radius is 5.2*s). Ships; visible at 10 px; copy it.
2. **`pop` can only ever animate a ghost, so two branches are dead code - and most deaths animate
   nothing at all.** The caller passes `pop = anim.life_pop if i == shown_lives else 0.0`
   (`ui.py:1084`) while `alive = i < shown_lives` (`ui.py:1083`) - the popped index is by construction
   the *first spent slot*, never an alive one. Consequences: (a) `s` is **always exactly 1.0** for
   every alive icon, and (b) the `0.3 * pop` term in the head-glow intensity is unreachable.
   Sharper still, index `shown_lives` is only *drawn* when `slots > shown_lives`, and
   `slots = max(3, shown_lives)`, so the pop is visible **only when the new `shown_lives` is 0, 1 or
   2**:

   | lives after the hit | `slots` | icons | popped index | drawn? |
   |---|---|---|---|---|
   | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | no - all four icons alive, nothing animates |
   | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 | no |
   | 2 | 3 | 3 | 2 | **yes** - ghost at index 2 |
   | 1 | 3 | 3 | 1 | **yes** |
   | 0 | 3 | 3 | 0 | **yes** |

   So on Easy (5 lives) the first two deaths pop nothing; only the last three lives animate. A life
   *gain* (which in the shipped game only happens at a level boundary, `gameplay.py:468`) pops nothing
   either, for the same reason. Net visible behaviour: the ghost of the life you just lost swells to
   1.35x and settles over 0.4167 s. Keep the formulas as written (they cost nothing) and keep the
   comment.

**TS home:** `web/src/ui/hud/LifeRow.ts`, `export class LifeRow` owning a `Container`, built once with
6 pooled `LifeIconView`s (1 glow sprite + 4 body sprites + 1 eye sprite each, added in that order) and
`update(theme, lives, t, lifePop)` toggling `visible`. Six, not five: the unreachable 6th slot costs
one hidden view and removes a branch.

**Cached texture + tint, not `Graphics`.** The four discs and the eye are hard-edged filled circles;
`textures.ts` has no such primitive yet - `discTexture` (`textures.ts:300-327`) is the *soft-edged*
shadow disc (full strength to 55% of the radius, then a linear fade over the outer 45%) and is the
wrong shape, and `whiteTexture()` is 1x1. Add to `web/src/gfx/textures.ts`:

```
/** One white hard-edged master disc, always scaled down by the sprite. */
export function solidCircleTexture(): Texture   // no radius argument - see §8.10.2
```

and drive it like `setGlow` does (`textures.ts:272-286`): `sprite.width/height` = the exact float
diameter, `tint = toHex(col)`, `alpha = 1`, default blend, `anchor 0.5`. Register it in
`disposeGeneratedTextures` / `generatedTextureStats` (`textures.ts:567-587`) like every other cache.

The head glow does **not** use `glowSprite`/`setGlow`: those bake `background.py::_radial`'s `1-f²`
falloff, which is a fundamentally fatter halo than ui.py's `(1-f)**2.4` (4x the brightness at half
radius - see §1.7's comparison table and §8.7). Use §1.7's `uiGlowTexture(radius)` through §1.8's
promoted `stampGlow`, with the normalised alpha from the bullet above.

### 8.2 `_draw_effect_icon(surface, kind, cx, cy, r, col, fonts, t)` - `ui.py:841-878`

Its docstring claims "tiny vector glyphs - no font dependency". That is **false for `double`**, which
is text (`ui.py:862-864`). The whole body is wrapped in `try/except: pass` (`ui.py:845`, `877-878`),
so a font failure silently draws a chip with no glyph rather than crashing.

`ix, iy = int(cx), int(cy)` (`ui.py:844`) - a no-op in practice, since every chip centre is integral
(§8.0). The only call site passes `r = size * 0.30` with `size = 26` -> **`r` is always exactly 7.8**
(`ui.py:906`); `col` arrives pre-whitened (§8.4); `fonts` is used only by `double`; and `t` is accepted
but **never used**, so the glyph is fully static and bakeable. No parameter has a default.

Exact construction per kind. `w` below is pygame's line width; every column shows the source
expression and its value at r = 7.8. Point lists are `(dx, dy)` offsets from `(ix, iy)`, y down.

| kind | line | primitive | geometry (source) | at r = 7.8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shield` | 846-849 | `draw.polygon(..., 2)` closed outline | `(0,-r) (0.92r,-0.42r) (0.6r,0.85r) (0,r) (-0.6r,0.85r) (-0.92r,-0.42r)` | `(0,-7.8) (7.176,-3.276) (4.68,6.63) (0,7.8) (-4.68,6.63) (-7.176,-3.276)` |
| `magnet` | 850-857 | `draw.arc` + 2 `draw.line` | box `Rect(0,0,int(2r),int(2r))`, `box.center = (ix, iy+int(0.3r))`, arc `0.0 .. pi`, `w = max(2,int(0.4r))`; prongs at `x0 = ix + sx*int(0.78r)` from `iy+int(0.3r)` to `iy+int(0.95r)`, same `w` | box 15x15 at topleft `(ix-7, iy-5)`; `w = 3`; prongs `x = ix +/- 6`, `y = iy+2 .. iy+7` |
| `slow` | 858-861 | `draw.circle(...,2)` + 2 `draw.line(...,2)` | ring radius `int(r)`; hand up `(ix,iy)->(ix, iy-int(0.62r))`; hand right `(ix,iy)->(ix+int(0.5r), iy)` | ring 7; up 4; right 3 |
| `double` | 862-864 | `draw_text` | `"x2"`, `_font(fonts,"tiny",14)`, centre-aligned, **top edge** at `iy - 0.95r`, `shadow=False` | top edge `iy - 7.41` |
| `ghost` | 865-870 | `draw.circle(...,2)` + `draw.lines(open, 2)` | head: centre `(ix, iy-int(0.15r))`, radius `int(0.8r)`; hem polyline `(-0.8r,0) (-0.8r,0.6r) (-0.35r,0.25r) (0,0.6r) (0.35r,0.25r) (0.8r,0.6r) (0.8r,0)` | head centre `(ix, iy-1)`, radius 6; hem `(-6.24,0) (-6.24,4.68) (-2.73,1.95) (0,4.68) (2.73,1.95) (6.24,4.68) (6.24,0)` |
| `frenzy` | 871-874 | `draw.polygon` **filled** | `(0.25r,-r) (-0.55r,0.15r) (-0.05r,0.15r) (-0.3r,r) (0.6r,-0.2r) (0.05r,-0.2r)` | `(1.95,-7.8) (-4.29,1.17) (-0.39,1.17) (-2.34,7.8) (4.68,-1.56) (0.39,-1.56)` |
| *(unknown)* | 875-876 | `draw.circle(..., 2)` | radius `max(2, int(0.6r))` | 4 |

Notes that change pixels:

- **`pygame.draw.arc` uses the mathematical y-up convention**, so `0.0 .. pi` is the **top** half of
  the box on screen (the same exception flagged in `entities.md` S1.9). The magnet is a horseshoe
  opening downward.
- **The magnet's horseshoe is half a pixel off its prongs, and the prongs are inset from its tips.**
  Get this right before transcribing: `int(2r) = int(15.6) = 15` is **odd**, and `Rect.center`'s
  setter floor-divides, so `box.center = (ix, iy+2)` produces `Rect(ix-7, iy-5, 15, 15)`. That box's
  *pixel* span is symmetric (`ix-7 .. ix+7`), but its exclusive right/bottom edges are `ix+8, iy+10`,
  so the **inscribed ellipse's centre is `(ix+0.5, iy+2.5)`** - half a pixel down and right of the
  `(ix, iy+2)` the code asked for, and half a pixel off the prong pair, which is centred on `ix`
  exactly. On top of that the prongs stand at `ix +/- int(0.78r) = ix +/- 6` while the arc's tips reach
  `ix-7` / `ix+7`, so each prong is inset ~1..1.5 px from the tip it is meant to continue. Ships: the
  3 px strokes (arc `w = 3` grows inward from the ellipse, prong lines are 3 px centred on `x0`) overlap
  enough that the joins read as continuous. Do not "fix" it - transcribe `int(2r)`, `int(0.3r)`,
  `int(0.78r)`, `int(0.95r)` and the floor-divided centring, i.e. bake the arc about `(cx+0.5, cy+2.5)`
  and the prongs about `cx`.
- `pygame.draw.circle(..., radius, width)` grows the ring **inward** from `radius` (the convention
  already tabulated in `entities.md` S6 and `render.md` S7). These glyphs are baked onto a **canvas**,
  which centres a stroke on its path and has no `alignment`, so path them at `radius - width/2`:
  `slow`'s ring at **6** with `lineWidth 2`, `ghost`'s head at **5**, the unknown-kind circle at **3**.
  (For the live-`Graphics` case - the combo rim, §8.5 - use the house idiom
  `stroke({ width: 2, alignment: 1 })` instead.) Polygon and polyline strokes are centred in both APIs;
  `pygame.draw.polygon(..., width=2)` is drawn as 2 px lines along the point list, so no adjustment
  there.
- `double` is the only glyph whose vertical anchor is the text *top edge*, so it hangs low. Measured on
  the ground-truth machine (§0.6.2): the `tiny` face renders `"x2"` as a **13 x 19** surface, ascent 16,
  with ink occupying rows **6..15** of it. `draw_text` truncates the anchor (`int(pos[1])`,
  `ui.py:281`), so with an integral `iy` the surface top lands at `iy - 8` and the ink runs
  `iy - 2 .. iy + 7` - centred **2.5 px below** the chip centre, inside a chip that spans
  `iy-13 .. iy+13`. Visibly low next to the other five, which are centred on `(ix, iy)`. Ships;
  reproduce it by anchoring the `Text` at `(0.5, 0)` on `y = cy - 7.41`, **not** by centring it.

#### 8.2.1 These are NOT the rune glyphs - `runeGlyphs.ts` cannot be reused as-is

`web/src/gfx/entities/runeGlyphs.ts` already bakes white glyph textures for the **world-space** runes,
transcribed from `powerups.py::_paint_glyph` (`powerups.py:226-273`). Those are a *different set of
drawings* by the same author. Five of six differ in kind, not just in size:

| kind | world (`_paint_glyph`) | HUD (`_draw_effect_icon`) | same shape? |
|---|---|---|---|
| magnet | horseshoe: `arcPts(0.92, 0, pi, 14)` polyline at `cy-0.10s`, prongs `+/-0.92s` down to `+0.74s`, **plus 2 polarity cross-bars** at `+0.74s` | `pygame.draw.arc` in a `2r` box centred `+0.3r`, prongs at `+/-0.78r` down to `+0.95r`, **no cross-bars** | no - different offsets, no caps, arc primitive vs polyline |
| shield | 5-point `_SHIELD` `(-0.80,-0.72)...`, **plus a vertical centre rib** | 6-point list `(0,-r) (0.92r,-0.42r)...`, no rib | **no - different point count** |
| slow | **hourglass** `_HOURGLASS` + grain dot at `+0.52s` | **clock**: circle + hands at 12 and 3 | **no - unrelated glyph** |
| double | **two nested diamonds** (scales 0.94, 0.48) | the literal **text `"x2"`** | **no - not even vector art** |
| ghost | dome `arcPts(0.74,0,pi,12)` + reversed `_GHOST_TAIL` as **one closed outline**, **plus 2 eye dots** | separate circle (r `0.8r`, centre `-0.15r`) + **open** 7-point hem, **no eyes** | no |
| frenzy | filled `_BOLT` `(0.16,-0.96) (-0.66,0.14) (-0.10,0.14) (-0.30,0.96) (0.62,-0.18) (0.06,-0.18)` | filled `(0.25,-1.0) (-0.55,0.15) (-0.05,0.15) (-0.30,1.0) (0.60,-0.20) (0.05,-0.20)` | **near-identical, still not equal** - two hand-tuned copies of one bolt |
| unknown | `circle(max(2,int(0.7s)), w)` | `circle(max(2,int(0.6r)), 2)` | different radius factor and width |

**Recommendation.** Do not fold these into `runeGlyphTexture` and do not write a second stroking
engine either. Promote the (already correct, already tested) path helpers out of `runeGlyphs.ts` into
a shared module and let both glyph tables use them:

- move `arcPts`, `strokePath`, `fillPath`, `line`, `dot`, `glyphSizeBucket` and the
  `lineCap="butt" / lineJoin="miter"` canvas setup from `runeGlyphs.ts:62-136`, `:211-213`, `:232-239`
  into **`web/src/gfx/glyphPaths.ts`**, re-exported so `runeGlyphs.ts` keeps its public API unchanged;
- add **`web/src/ui/hud/hudGlyphs.ts`** with `paintHudGlyph(ctx, kind, cx, cy, r)` (the table above)
  and `export function hudGlyphTexture(kind: string, r: number): Texture`, cache key
  **`` `${kind}|${rBucket}` ``** where `rBucket = Math.round(r * 4) / 4` (r is a constant 7.8 today, so
  the cache is 6 entries; bucketing keeps it 6 if a later screen asks for a different size).

Mirror the `int()`/`Math.trunc` calls that appear *inside* the Python glyph expressions (the magnet
box and offsets, the slow hands, both circle radii, the stroke widths). They are authored - the
runeGlyphs port already keeps them (`runeGlyphs.ts:145`, `:173`, `:192`, `:212`) - and at r = 7.8
dropping them lengthens the slow hand by 20%. Widget *positions* stay float per the settled decision.

`double` cannot be a baked vector: it needs a `Text` at the `tiny` face - `fonts.tiny = get(14)`
(`fonts.py:69`), i.e. the UI face at 14 px, whose web stack §0.8.3 chooses - centred horizontally with
its **top** at `cy - 7.41` (`anchor (0.5, 0)`). Simplest faithful port: give `ChipView` an optional
`Text` child used only by `double`, and leave its glyph sprite hidden. It is the one kind whose glyph
depends on the FontBook port; nothing else in this section needs a font except the combo number (§8.5).
Since `double` is one of only six glyphs and the string is constant, a build-time bake of the *rendered*
`"x2"` into the glyph texture is **not** an option worth taking: it would freeze whichever face the
browser resolved at bake time.

**Blend and colour.** The glyph is drawn by `pygame.draw.*` straight onto the frame, i.e. **opaque
replace, normal blend, no antialiasing** - not additive. Port it as a normal-blend sprite,
`alpha = 1`, `tint = toHex(lerpColor(col, UI_WHITE, 0.35 * beat))` (§8.4). The baked texture's edges
are antialiased where pygame's are hard; that is the same softening already accepted for every other
baked glyph.

### 8.3 `_chip_bg(size, col)` - `ui.py:881-889`

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Surface | `pygame.Surface((size, size), SRCALPHA)`, i.e. 26x26 |
| Fill | `draw.rect(with_alpha(UI_PANEL, 225), (0,0,size,size), border_radius=8)` |
| Rim | `draw.rect(with_alpha(col, 150), (0,0,size,size), width=1, border_radius=8)` |
| Cache | `_MISC_CACHE`, key `("chip", size, col)`; `_store` wipes the whole cache at 384 entries (`ui.py:63-68`) |
| Blit | `surface.blit(bg, (int(cx - 13), int(cy - 13)))`, normal alpha blend (`ui.py:905`) |

`col` is the already-`_q`-quantised kind colour, and there are six of them at one size, so this cache
holds at most 6 entries and never churns. `size` is only ever 26 and `border_radius` only ever 8, well
under pygame's clamp of half the shorter side (13), so the corner is a true 8 px quarter-circle. The
1 px rim is `width=1`, which grows **inward** from the box edge, i.e. it occupies the outermost pixel
ring - no adjustment needed when the port paths it inset by `width/2`.

**Hazard: the rim replaces, it does not blend.** `pygame.draw.rect` with an RGBA colour on an
`SRCALPHA` surface **overwrites** destination RGBA. The rim pixels are therefore exactly
`(col, alpha 150)` with the panel fill *gone* underneath them; Canvas 2D (and a second Pixi sprite)
would composite `rim over fill`. The arithmetic difference here is bounded by
`0.412 * (panel*0.882 - backdrop)`, and the backdrop under the chip *is* the HUD strip's own dark
panel gradient, so the discrepancy is under ~4/255 per channel. **Accept it; do not build a
RenderTexture to chase it.**

**TS home:** `web/src/ui/hud/chipTextures.ts` - two cached **white** textures, colour by tint:

| Export | Texture | Key | Sprite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `chipFillTexture(size, corner)` | filled rounded rect, white | `` `${size}|${corner}` `` | `tint = UI_PANEL`, `alpha = 225/255 = 0.88235` |
| `chipRimTexture(size, corner, width)` | rounded-rect **stroke**, white, path inset by `width/2` so it stays inside the box like pygame's | `` `${size}|${corner}|${width}` `` | `tint = toHex(col)`, `alpha = 150/255 = 0.58824` |

Two textures for the entire game (26/8 and 26/8/1) against Python's six surfaces. Use
`ctx.roundRect(0.5, 0.5, 25, 25, 8)` for the rim, `lineWidth = 1`.

### 8.4 `_draw_effect_chip(surface, cx, cy, kind, remaining, theme, fonts, t)` - `ui.py:892-925`

**There is no seconds text anywhere on the chip.** The dot ring is the only timer; `POWERUP_TYPES`'
`icon` strings ("M", "S", "T", "2x", "G", "F") are never printed by the HUD despite the comment at
`powerups.py:48-49`. Do not add a countdown number to the port.

Resolved inputs (`ui.py:894-897`):

| Symbol | Formula | Line |
|---|---|---|
| `info` | `_powerup_types().get(kind, {})` | 894 |
| `col` | `_q(info.get("color") or theme.accent2)` | 895 |
| `dur` | `float(info.get("duration") or POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION) or 1.0` | 896 |
| `frac` | `clamp(remaining / dur, 0.0, 1.0)` | 897 |
| `urgent` | `remaining <= 2.0` | 900 |
| `beat` | `0.5 + 0.5 * pulse(t, 16.0)` if urgent else `1.0` -> **range [0.5, 1.0]** | 901 |
| `size` | `26` | 902 |

`_powerup_types()` (`ui.py:679-690`) merges `POWERUP_TYPES` **over** the local `_PU_FALLBACK`
(`ui.py:668-675`) with `merged.setdefault(k, {}).update(dict(v))`, so the fallback's colours and
`POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION` are **dead constants** whenever `core.powerups` imports (always). The
effective table is:

| kind | `col` (`powerups.py:50-93`) | `dur` | seconds per ring dot (`dur/24`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| magnet | `(255, 92, 96)` | 8.0 | 0.3333 |
| shield | `(86, 220, 255)` | 12.0 | 0.5 |
| slow | `(144, 124, 255)` | 6.5 | 0.2708 |
| double | `(255, 208, 84)` | 10.0 | 0.4167 |
| ghost | `(214, 228, 255)` | 6.0 | 0.25 |
| frenzy | `(255, 74, 190)` | 8.0 | 0.3333 |

Both already exist in TS: `powerupColor(kind)` / `powerupDuration(kind)`
(`web/src/core/powerups.ts:215`, `:223`), and the TS table (`powerups.ts:74-117`) is value-identical to
`powerups.py:50-93`, icon strings included. **One divergence, defensive only:** for an *unknown* kind
Python falls back to `theme.accent2` and `POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION` (7.0), while `powerupInfo`'s
fallback row uses `UI_WHITE` (`powerups.ts:203-212`). Resolve the colour in the chip rather than via
`powerupColor` if you want strict parity, but note the branch is **unreachable in the shipped game**:
the only grant site is `effects.add(rune.kind)` (`gameplay.py:778`, `GameplayWorld.ts:624`) and
`rune.kind` is always one of the six.

`frac` uses the **table** duration, not `ActiveEffects.fraction()` (which divides by `_totals`, the
largest duration ever granted for that kind). Because that same single grant site never passes a custom
`duration`, and `add` clamps with `left = max(existing, dur)` (`powerups.py:734-746`), `remaining` can
never exceed the table `dur` and the two agree **exactly** in the shipped game. Keep the table division
anyway - it is what Python does, and it keeps the chip independent of `_totals`.

**Draw order** (back to front) and exact parameters:

| # | Line | Thing | Parameters |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 903 | additive glow | `_blit_glow(cx, cy, radius=26, col, intensity=(0.30 + 0.30*pulse(t, 2.4)) * beat)` -> 0.30..0.60 idle, x`beat` when urgent |
| 2 | 904-905 | chip background | `_chip_bg(26, col)` blitted at `(cx-13, cy-13)` |
| 3 | 906-907 | glyph | `_draw_effect_icon(kind, cx, cy, r=7.8, lerp_color(col, UI_WHITE, 0.35*beat))` |
| 4 | 911-921 | 24 ring dots | see below |
| 5 | 922-925 | leading-dot glow | `_blit_glow(cx + cos(ang)*17, cy + sin(ang)*17, radius=7, col, 0.8*beat)` where `ang = -pi/2 + TAU*(lit/24)`; **skipped when `lit == 0`** |

The breathe is `pulse(t, 2.4)` = 0.382 Hz (period 2.618 s) on the **absolute** clock, so every chip
breathes in phase. The urgency strobe is `pulse(t, 16.0)` = 2.546 Hz (period 0.3927 s), also absolute,
so it starts mid-phase at the moment `remaining` crosses 2.0 - the threshold is a hard switch with no
ramp (`ui.py:900`).

**The countdown ring** (`ui.py:909-921`): `ring_r = 26*0.5 + 4.0 = 17.0`, `segs = 24`,
`lit = int(round(24 * frac))`. Dot i sits at `ang = -pi/2 + TAU*(i/24)` - 15 degrees per dot, starting
at 12 o'clock, running **clockwise on screen** (y is down, so no sign flip is needed). The ring, not the
chip, is what defines the row's pitch: `ring_r + lit dot radius = 19`, and the chip pitch is 38, so
adjacent chips' rings are exactly **tangent**, and the rightmost chip's ring touches the 1260 right
margin. At `frac = 1` (`lit = 24`) the leading-dot glow's angle wraps to `-pi/2` and it sits at 12
o'clock **on top of dot 0**.

| dot | condition | colour | radius |
|---|---|---|---|
| lit | `i < lit` | `lerp_color(col, UI_WHITE, 0.25 * beat)` | 2 |
| spent | `i >= lit` | `shade(col, 0.22)` | 1 |

Python's `round()` is banker's rounding and JS's `Math.round` is half-up; `24*frac` lands exactly on
.5 for one float value per dot boundary, so use `Math.round` and forget it.

**What urgency actually does** (all via `beat`, which only ever halves things - it never desaturates
to grey and never hides anything):

| Element | non-urgent | urgent range |
|---|---|---|
| halo intensity | `0.30 + 0.30*pulse(t,2.4)` | x`[0.5, 1.0]` -> as low as 0.15 |
| glyph whiteness | `lerp(col, WHITE, 0.35)` | `lerp(col, WHITE, [0.175, 0.35])` |
| lit dot whiteness | `lerp(col, WHITE, 0.25)` | `lerp(col, WHITE, [0.125, 0.25])` |
| leading glow | `0.8` | `[0.4, 0.8]` |
| chip bg, spent dots | unchanged | unchanged |

**Chip ordering is by time remaining, not by age.** `state["effects"] = list(self.effects.items())`
(`gameplay.py:1250`) and `ActiveEffects.items()` returns `sorted(..., key=lambda kv: kv[1])`
(`powerups.py:800-802`) - **ascending remaining**. So the chip about to expire is always leftmost and
chips visibly **swap places** when their timers cross. The comment "newest on the right"
(`ui.py:1111`) is wrong. `web/src/core/powerups.ts:701-703` already sorts identically (and both
languages' sorts are stable, so ties keep insertion order); the port must consume `items()` in that
order, must not add position tweening, and must key its pooled views by **slot index** (so a swap is two
tint/texture writes, not a reparent).

**TS home:** `web/src/ui/hud/EffectChips.ts` - `export class EffectChipRow` owning a `Container` and 6
pooled `ChipView`s, `update(effects: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, number]>, theme, t)`.

| Part | Implementation | Key / note |
|---|---|---|
| halo | `Sprite` on `uiGlowTexture(26)` (§1.7), alpha `(0.30 + 0.30*pulse(t,2.4))*beat*0.9102` | 26 bands -> normalisation `(1-1/26)**2.4 = 0.9102`; alpha <= **0.546**, so one sprite, tint x alpha exact |
| background | 2 sprites, `chipFillTexture` + `chipRimTexture` | §8.3 |
| glyph | `Sprite(hudGlyphTexture(kind, 7.8))`, normal blend, `alpha 1`, tinted | `` `${kind}|7.8` `` |
| glyph (`double`) | `Text("x2")` at the `tiny` face, `anchor (0.5, 0)`, y = `cy - 7.41` | the one font dependency |
| ring, lit arc | one `Graphics`, geometry rebuilt **only when `lit` changes**, colour as its `tint` | rebuild every `dur/24` s = 0.25 (ghost) .. 0.5 (shield) |
| ring, spent arc | second `Graphics`, same rebuild trigger, static tint `shade(col,0.22)` | two Graphics because lit/spent differ in *both* colour and radius, which one tint cannot express |
| leading glow | `Sprite` on `uiGlowTexture(7)`, alpha `0.8*beat*0.6907`, `visible = lit > 0` | 7 bands -> `(1-1/7)**2.4 = 0.6907`; alpha <= **0.5526** |

144 individually pooled dot sprites would also work; the two-`Graphics` split is fewer objects and
rebuilds ~4x/second per chip instead of every frame.

Note both glow rows use `uiGlowTexture` + §1.8's promoted `stampGlow`, **not** `setGlow`/`radialTexture`
- see §8.7's falloff row. The `0.9102` / `0.6907` factors are not fudge: they are the peak band of
Python's `_glow_add` at those radii (`(1 - 1/bands)**2.4`, §1.7), which the normalised white texture
divides out.

### 8.5 `_draw_combo(surface, cx, cy, combo, anim, theme, fonts, t)` - `ui.py:928-957`

**Appears only when `combo > 1`** (`ui.py:1091`); at combo 0 or 1 the HUD draws the static caption
`"COMBO"` in `shade(dim, 0.7)`, centred at `(812, 33)` instead. `combo` is clamped to
`COMBO_MAX = 8` by the sim (`gameplay.py:799`), so the badge only ever shows x2..x8.

| Symbol | Formula | Line | Values |
|---|---|---|---|
| `steps` | `max(1, int(COMBO_MAX))` | 931 | 8 |
| `heat` | `clamp(combo / steps, 0, 1)` | 932 | 0.25, 0.375, ... 1.0 |
| `col` | `rainbow(t * 0.6)` if `combo >= steps` else `lerp_color(_q(theme.accent), UI_GOLD, heat)` | 933 | see below |
| `pop` | `clamp(anim.combo_pop, 0, 1)` | 934 | 1 -> 0 over 0.3333 s |
| `scale` | `1.0 + 0.45 * ease_out_back(pop)` | 936 | see below |

**Colour ramp.** combo 2..7 interpolate the level accent toward `UI_GOLD (255,208,84)` by `heat`; at
combo 8 the badge switches to `rainbow(t*0.6)` = `hsv((t*0.6) % 1, 0.85, 1.0)`, a full hue cycle every
**1.6667 s**. `UI_GOLD` and `rainbow` both already exist (`palette.ts:297`, `:116`).

**The pop is not monotonic.** `ease_out_back` overshoots (`contracts.py:207-211`), and `pop` sweeps
1 -> 0, so `scale` *rises* after the trigger before settling:

| `pop` | `ease_out_back(pop)` | `scale` | `int(17*scale)` | `int(26*scale)` | when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1.00 | 1.0000 | 1.4500 | 24 | 37 | t = 0 (the increment) |
| 0.5801 | 1.1000 | **1.4950** | 25 | 38 | t = 0.1400 s (**peak**; `eob` is stationary at `pop = 1 - 3.40316/8.10474`) |
| 0.30 | 0.9071 | 1.4082 | 23 | 36 | t = 0.2333 s |
| 0.00 | 0.0000 | 1.0000 | 17 | 26 | t = 0.3333 s |

`ease_out_back` in TS is `easeOutBack` (`mathx.ts:65`), same `c1 = 1.70158, c3 = 2.70158`.

Layers, back to front:

| # | Line | Thing | Parameters |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 938 | additive glow | `_blit_glow(cx, cy, radius = 26*scale, col, 0.35 + 0.55*heat + 0.5*pop)`; `_blit_glow` truncates the radius, so the texture steps 26 -> 38 |
| 2 | 939 | badge body | filled circle, `UI_PANEL`, radius `int(17*scale)` - **opaque**, it is a hole punched in the strip |
| 3 | 940 | badge rim | circle outline, `col`, radius `int(17*scale)`, width 2 (grows inward, so it occupies radius 15..17 at rest) |
| 4 | 943-948 | 8 pips | `ang = -pi/2 + TAU*(i/8)`, at a **constant** radius `24.0`; `on = i < combo` -> colour `col` radius 2, else `shade(col, 0.20)` radius 1 |
| 5 | 950-957 | the number | `f"x{combo}"` |

- **Glow intensity exceeds 1.0 - but not for the reason you would guess.** `0.35 + 0.55*heat + 0.5*pop`
  reaches **1.40** at combo 8 mid-pop (idle values: 0.4875 at x2 ... 0.90 at x8). Two separate things
  follow, and only one of them matters:
  1. *Python's per-band saturation is invisible here.* `_glow_add` bakes `shade(col, b)` per band with
     per-channel `clamp8`, so bands with `b > 1` **saturate toward white**. That happens where
     `(1-f)**2.4 * 1.4 > 1`, i.e. `f < 0.1309` - the inner 13% of a 38 px radius, so within **5 px of
     the centre**. The badge body drawn immediately afterwards is *opaque* `UI_PANEL` out to
     `int(17*scale) = 25` px, so every saturated pixel is painted over. The `shade(c, f>1)` exception
     to the "colour leaves the texture" rule therefore never shows on this widget; do **not** bake a
     colour-specific texture for it.
  2. *Clamping the sprite alpha would dim the whole halo.* What is actually visible is the annulus
     between the body edge and the glow rim: at peak pop, `f` from `25/38 = 0.658` to 1, i.e.
     `b <= (1-0.658)**2.4 * 1.4 = 0.098`. Nothing there saturates - it is a plain linear add. But with
     §1.7's normalised texture the sprite alpha is `i * 0.9102 = 1.274`, and `setGlow`'s clamp
     (`textures.ts:285`) would cut it to 1.0, making the *entire* visible halo **21.5% dimmer** than
     Python. So still **stamp two additive sprites** (§1.7's escape hatch): `alpha = min(1, a)` and
     `alpha = max(0, a - 1)` with `a = (0.35 + 0.55*heat + 0.5*pop) * 0.9102`. They sum linearly in the
     dim annulus and saturate identically in the hidden core (worked check, `col = UI_GOLD`, `a = 1.274`
     at the peak band: Python `shade(UI_GOLD, 1.274) = (255, clamp8(265)=255, 107)`; two sprites
     `(255,208,84) + (70,57,23)` clipped -> `(255,255,107)`). The second sprite is only ever needed when
     `a > 1`, i.e. `0.35 + 0.55*heat + 0.5*pop > 1.0987` -> **combo >= 4 and `pop > 0.397`**: set
     `visible = a > 1` and it costs nothing for most of the game.
- **The pips do not scale with the badge.** Their radius is a literal `24.0` while the badge rim grows
  to `int(17*1.4947) = 25`, so at peak pop the rim crosses *outside* the pip ring and the pips (drawn
  after, on top) read as beads sitting on the rim rather than orbiting it. Ships; **ported as a
  quirk** - do not multiply 24.0 by `scale`.
- **The number.** `_text_pair(f"x{combo}", _font(fonts, "h2", 26, True), lerp_color(col, UI_WHITE, 0.45))`
  and the silhouette is **discarded** (`_sil`), so the combo number is the one HUD string with **no
  drop shadow**. `_font(fonts, "h2", 26, True)` returns `fonts.h2`, which is `get(30, bold=True)`
  (`fonts.py:66`) - **30 px bold**; the 26 is only the no-FontBook fallback size (and `_sys_font`
  would then build `Font(None, int(26*1.15)) = 29`).
- **`pygame.transform.rotozoom(body, 0.0, scale)`** (`ui.py:952-956`) runs whenever
  `abs(scale-1) > 0.02`, i.e. `pop > 0.0096` - all but the final ~3 ms of the pop. Angle 0 -> this is a **plain
  uniform scale of already-rasterised text**, not a re-render at a larger point size: the glyphs go
  soft at 1.49x. In Pixi, set `text.scale = scale` and **never** touch `style.fontSize`, which would
  re-rasterise and come out crisp - a different look. rotozoom grows the surface and the blit
  re-centres on the new size (`ui.py:957`), so centring is preserved; `anchor.set(0.5)` reproduces it.

**TS home:** `web/src/ui/hud/ComboBadge.ts`, `export class ComboBadge`, `update(combo, comboPop, theme, t)`.

| Part | Implementation |
|---|---|
| glow | 2 `Sprite`s via `setGlow(s, 26*scale, col, ...)`, the overdrive split above |
| body | `Sprite(solidCircleTexture(...))`, tint `UI_PANEL`, `alpha 1`, normal blend, size `2*int(17*scale)` |
| rim | **live `Graphics`** - the one exception in this section. A cached annulus texture scaled by `scale` would fatten the 2 px rim to 2.9 px at peak pop, and the radius changes every frame during a pop anyway. Rebuild only while `pop > 0`; otherwise leave the geometry alone and write `tint` |
| pips | 8 `Sprite`s on `solidCircleTexture`, tint + size per frame, positions fixed at radius 24 |
| number | one `Text`, `anchor 0.5`, position `(cx, cy)`, `scale = scale`, tint from `lerpColor(col, UI_WHITE, 0.45)` |

Pixi's `Text` line-box metrics are not pygame's `Font.render` surface height, so the vertical centring
of "x8" may sit a pixel or two off Python. Check it perceptually against `captures/` rather than
computing an offset.

### 8.6 Where every constant comes from

| Constant | Source in the port |
|---|---|
| `COMBO_MAX` (8) | **exists**: `config.json` -> `C.COMBO_MAX` (`config.ts:112`) |
| `START_LIVES` (3) | **exists**: `C.START_LIVES` (`config.ts:127`) |
| `POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION` (7.0) | **exists**: `C.POWERUP_DEFAULT_DURATION` (`config.ts:122`) |
| `WINDOW_W` (1280), `HUD_H` (78) | **exists**: `config.ts:53`, `:57` |
| per-kind colour / duration | **exists**: `powerupColor` / `powerupDuration` (`core/powerups.ts:215`, `:223`) - but see the `theme.accent2` unknown-kind divergence in §8.4 |
| `UI_PANEL`, `UI_WHITE`, `UI_DIM`, `UI_GOLD` | **exists**: `palette.ts:281-297` |
| `shade`, `lerpColor`, `withAlpha`, `rainbow`, `toHex` | **exists**: `palette.ts:36-123` |
| `bodyAt(theme, f)` | **exists**: `palette.ts:175` |
| `clamp`, `pulse`, `easeOutBack`, `TAU` | **exists**: `core/mathx.ts:10-74` |
| every layout/geometry number in §8.0-§8.5 (636, 654, 44, 812, 40, 38, 50, 26, 17.0, 24.0, 24, 5.6, 5.2, 2.4, 6.4, 3.4, 2.2, 0.8, 1.7, 0.35, 0.45, 2.0, 16.0, 2.4, 0.30, 0.22, 0.20, 8, 225, 150, ...) | **must be added, as module-local `const`s in the `ui/hud/*` file that uses them, each with its `ui.py:LINE`.** They are literals in `ui.py` and are absent from `config.py`, so they are **not** in `config.json` and `tools/export_data.py` has nothing to export. Do **not** hand-edit `config.json` to add them - that would put layout in the balance export and break the "generated file" contract |

### 8.7 pygame-vs-Pixi hazards specific to this section

| Hazard | Where it bites | Handling |
|---|---|---|
| `BLEND_RGB_ADD` ignores src alpha; falloff is baked into RGB | every `_blit_glow` here | the shared `stampGlow`/`setGlow` convention already covers it |
| additive intensity > 1 saturates per channel toward white | combo glow, up to 1.40 | two stacked glow sprites (§8.5) - `setGlow` alone clamps and under-brightens |
| per-surface `set_alpha` / RGBA `draw.rect` **replaces** instead of blending | `_chip_bg`'s 1 px rim over its own fill | accept the <=4/255 difference (§8.3) |
| `pygame.draw.*` is opaque and **unantialiased** | life discs, all glyphs, ring dots, badge body/rim, pips | baked/`Graphics` output is antialiased; accepted softening, same as every other glyph in the port |
| circle outline `width` grows **inward** from `radius` | slow ring (7), ghost head (6), unknown (4), badge rim (`int(17*scale)`) | path at `radius - width/2` (6, 5, 3, `int(17*scale) - 1`) |
| `pygame.draw.arc` uses **y-up** math angles | magnet only | `0..pi` is the **top** half on screen |
| surface caches keyed on quantised values | `_glow_add` keys on `_q8(colour)` (32 levels/channel) + intensity to 0.1 + `int(radius)`; `_text_pair` keys on `_q8(colour)` | the port is smooth; at combo 8 Python's rainbow glow *and digits* step through a 32-level grid while the rim and pips use the exact colour. The port's uniform smoothness is the already-accepted deviation - do not re-introduce `_q8` |
| integer blit truncation | every `_blit_glow` and `blit` position | float coords per the settled decision |
| `int()` inside authored glyph geometry | magnet box/offsets, slow hands, glyph radii, stroke widths | **keep** the truncation inside glyph builders (precedent: `runeGlyphs.ts:145`, `:173`, `:192`) |
| `rotozoom(surf, 0.0, scale)` is a bitmap scale of rendered text | combo number | scale the display object, never `fontSize` |
| `SRCALPHA` scratch then normal blit | `_chip_bg` only; everything else in this section draws straight onto the frame | - |

### 8.8 Module summary

| New file | Exports |
|---|---|
| `web/src/gfx/glyphPaths.ts` | `arcPts`, `strokePath`, `fillPath`, `line`, `dot`, `glyphSizeBucket` - **moved** out of `gfx/entities/runeGlyphs.ts`, which re-exports them so its API is unchanged |
| `web/src/gfx/textures.ts` (extend, do not add a second cache) | `solidCircleTexture(radius)` - hard-edged white disc, key `quantiseRadius(radius)`, registered in `disposeGeneratedTextures` / `generatedTextureStats` |
| `web/src/ui/hud/hudGlyphs.ts` | `paintHudGlyph`, `hudGlyphTexture(kind, r)` (key `` `${kind}|${rBucket}` ``), `HUD_GLYPH_R = 7.8` |
| `web/src/ui/hud/chipTextures.ts` | `chipFillTexture(size, corner)`, `chipRimTexture(size, corner, width)` |
| `web/src/ui/hud/LifeRow.ts` | `class LifeRow` |
| `web/src/ui/hud/EffectChips.ts` | `class EffectChipRow`, `class ChipView` (private) |
| `web/src/ui/hud/ComboBadge.ts` | `class ComboBadge` |

`_HudAnim` itself is shared with the score odometer and the goal bar, so it belongs to the HUD
container (`web/src/ui/hud/Hud.ts`) specced in the HUD-layout section; the four fields these widgets
read are listed in §8.0.

### 8.9 Quirks, ported as quirks

1. **The life-icon eye does not follow the head's bob** - it is placed from the icon centre while the
   head disc bobs +/-2.2 px around it (`ui.py:828` vs `:838`). Visible at 10 px.
2. **`life_pop` can only animate a spent ghost**; alive icons are permanently at `s = 1.0` and the
   `0.3*pop` head-glow term is unreachable, and gaining a life pops nothing (`ui.py:824`, `:834`,
   `:1083-1085`).
3. **The magnet's prongs are inset ~1.5 px from its horseshoe tips** (`ui.py:851-857`).
4. **`double`'s glyph is text, not vector art**, contradicting the function's own docstring, and is
   the only glyph anchored by its top edge - it reads low in the chip (`ui.py:843`, `:862-864`).
5. **The whole glyph body is `try/except: pass`** - a font failure draws a chip with no glyph rather
   than raising (`ui.py:845`, `:877-878`).
6. **`_PU_FALLBACK`'s six colours and durations are dead** - the merge order lets `POWERUP_TYPES` win
   (`ui.py:668-690`). Do not port them as live values.
7. **The chip row is sorted by time remaining, so chips swap places mid-run**, and the comment
   claiming "newest on the right" is wrong (`ui.py:1111` vs `powerups.py:800-802`).
8. **The combo pips do not scale with the badge**, so the rim overtakes them at peak pop
   (`ui.py:939` vs `:945`).
9. **The combo number has no drop shadow** because `_text_pair`'s silhouette is discarded
   (`ui.py:950`).
10. **`scale` peaks 0.140 s *after* the combo increment** at 1.4950, not on the frame of it
    (`ease_out_back` overshoot, `ui.py:936`).
11. **The `+n` life overflow and the tighter `pitch` below 34 px are unreachable** at the shipped
    difficulty lives (1/2/3/5, monotonically decreasing) - `pitch` is only ever 34.0 or 28.0
    (`ui.py:1078-1088`).

### 8.10 Open questions

1. **Glyph texture resolution.** These are the smallest line drawings in the game: 2-3 px strokes
   inside a 26 px chip. Baking at 1 design px per texture px (the `textures.ts` house rule,
   `canvasTexture` `resolution: 1`) matches `runeGlyphs.ts` but will look mushy once the viewport
   upscales ~3x on a 4K display. Recommendation: ship 1x for parity, and if the perceptual check
   against `captures/` objects, bake at a single named `HUD_GLYPH_SUPERSAMPLE = 3` with the sprite
   still sized in design px. Needs a screenshot call, not an argument.
2. **`solidCircleTexture` bucketing.** `quantiseRadius` is exact below 8 px, which is the entire range
   the life row and the ring dots use, so a popping ghost crosses a bucket every ~1 px of radius
   growth. Confirm the texture swap is invisible (it should be: the sprite's `width/height` carry the
   exact float size either way), or pin one 32 px master disc and always downscale.
3. **The life-icon radii are float in the port** (5.2/4.4/3.6/2.8) where Python truncates to
   5/4/3/2 - the icons read ~10-18% fatter in radius. This follows the settled "no integer
   truncation" decision and the truncation is a pygame API artefact rather than authored intent, but
   it is the one place in this section where that decision is visible at a glance. If the perceptual
   check objects, the fix is a single `Math.trunc` at `ui/hud/LifeRow.ts`.
## 9. HUD: `draw_hud` layout, every coordinate

**Ground truth:** `snake/gfx/ui.py:960-1128` (`draw_hud`, `_draw_hud_impl`), plus
`snake/scenes/gameplay.py::_hud_state` for the producing side. Sections 7 and 8 specify the
widgets this one places (`_hud_backdrop`, `_draw_odometer`, `_draw_life_icon`,
`_draw_effect_chip`, `_draw_combo`) and §2-§4 the primitives (`draw_text`, `draw_bar`,
`_blit_glow`); this section owns only the **layout and the animation bookkeeping**.

This section was written directly from the source rather than by an agent, and it is the one
section of this document whose numbers were transcribed and re-read in the same pass.

### 9.1 `draw_hud` - the wrapper (ui.py:960-989)

Three duties, all worth keeping:

| Duty | Code | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Clip to the strip | `strip = Rect(0, 0, C.WINDOW_W, C.HUD_H)`, intersected with any existing clip (`strip.clip(prev_clip)`), then `surface.set_clip(strip)` | The backdrop bloom and every chip halo are additive and would otherwise reach **~100 px down into the play field**, brightening the top of the arena. The clip makes HUD and arena strictly disjoint, so they can be drawn in either order. |
| Restore the clip | `finally: surface.set_clip(prev_clip)`, itself wrapped in `try/except` | |
| Tolerate a bad state dict | `state or {}`, and every read goes through `num()` / `state.get` with a default | `draw_hud` ignores unknown keys and survives missing ones. §8.1 of integration.md requires the port keep this tolerance. |

> **Quirk - ported, but not as-is.** The whole body is wrapped in a bare `except Exception:
> pass` (ui.py:983-984). Any error anywhere in the HUD silently draws **nothing** and the game
> carries on. That is shipped behaviour, but a silent blank HUD is a bug that hides bugs. Port
> recommendation: keep the clip and the missing-key tolerance, and let exceptions propagate in
> development while catching-and-logging in production. Do not copy a silent `pass`.

**Clipping in Pixi.** The strip is a rectangular mask on the HUD root container, the same
device `GameplayScene` already uses for the arena (`arenaMask`). Because the strip is fixed at
`(0, 0, 1280, 78)` in design space, the mask is built once, never per frame.

### 9.2 Where `t` and `dt` come from - read this before touching the animation

`draw_hud(surface, game, state, theme, t)` is called with **`game.time`** - unscaled, real
elapsed time (integration.md §8, layer 9: "**unscaled `game.time`**"). The HUD then derives its
own `dt` by differencing that clock:

```python
dt = clamp(t - anim.last_t, 0.0, 0.1)     # ui.py:996
anim.last_t = t                            # ui.py:997
```

Consequences the port must preserve:

- The HUD is a **real-dt consumer**. Slow motion (`fx.time_scale()`) does not stretch the score
  roll-up, the combo pop or the boost bar. This is the deliberate "hit-stop with live UI" split
  of integration.md §10 - assert it in review.
- The clamp is **0.1 s**, not `MAX_DT` (0.05). A stall longer than 100 ms freezes the HUD
  animation for that frame rather than jumping it.
- `dt` is computed at *draw* time, not passed in. In the port the HUD view's `update(state, t)`
  should difference the same `game.time` value, so a frame that draws twice cannot double-step
  the animation.

### 9.3 State reads and their defaults (ui.py:999-1018)

`num(key, default)` coerces to `float` and falls back on any exception or `None`.

| Local | Source key | Default | Post-processing |
|---|---|---|---|
| `score` | `score` | 0 | `int()` |
| `highscore` | `highscore` | 0 | `int()` |
| `goal` | `goal_food` | 1 | `max(1, int(...))` - never divides by zero |
| `eaten` | `food_eaten` | 0 | `int()` |
| `lives` | `lives` | `C.START_LIVES` (3) | `int()` |
| `combo` | `combo` | 0 | `int()` |
| `boost` | `boost` | 0 | raw float (stamina, not a fraction) |
| `boost_max` | `boost_max` | `C.SNAKE_BOOST_MAX` (100) | `max(1.0, ...)` |
| `level_index` | `level_index` | 0 | `int()`, displayed `+1` |
| `level_name` | `level_name` | `theme.name` | `str(... or "")` - falls back to the **theme name**, then to empty |

`accent`, `accent2`, `dim` are `_q(theme.accent / .accent2 / .text_dim)`. `_q` is an int-cast
for hashability only (§1.4) and never alters a legal channel value; in TS these are just
`theme.accent`, `theme.accent2`, `theme.textDim`.

The state contract in integration.md §8.1 also lists `difficulty`, `difficulty_label`,
`difficulty_color`, `mode`, `chapter`, `chapter_title` and `beat_title`. **`_draw_hud_impl`
reads none of them.** They are produced by `_hud_state` and consumed elsewhere (the READY card,
§9 of integration.md). Do not add HUD elements for them.

### 9.4 Animation bookkeeping (ui.py:1020-1040)

Runs before any drawing, in this exact order. All four "pop" values are 0..1 and decay linearly;
only the score uses an exponential chase.

| Field | Trigger | Rise | Decay |
|---|---|---|---|
| `score_hit` | `score > score_disp + 0.5` | `min(1, score_hit + dt * 4.0)` | `max(0, score_hit - dt * 2.2)`, applied every frame **after** the rise |
| `score_disp` | always | `score_disp += (score - score_disp) * (1 - exp(-11.0 * dt))` | snaps: `if abs(score - score_disp) < 0.6: score_disp = score` |
| `combo_pop` | `combo > prev_combo` | set to `1.0` | `- dt * 3.0` |
| `food_pop` | `eaten > prev_food` | set to `1.0` | `- dt * 2.6` |
| `life_pop` | `prev_lives >= 0 and lives != prev_lives` | set to `1.0` | `- dt * 2.4` |

Three details that are easy to lose:

1. **`score_disp` uses a true exponential chase**, `1 - exp(-11·dt)`, not the `min(1, dt·k)`
   approximation used for the snake's bank (integration.md §10). It is therefore frame-rate
   independent and must **not** be "simplified" to a lerp.
2. **`life_pop` triggers on `!=`, not `<`** - gaining a life pops the row exactly as losing one
   does. The `prev_lives >= 0` guard exists so the first frame after construction
   (`prev_lives = -1`) does not pop.
3. `prev_combo` / `prev_food` / `prev_lives` are written every frame regardless of the branch.

`_hud_anim(game)` caches this record **on the game object** in Python (§7). The port puts it on
the HUD view object; do not monkey-patch `Game`.

### 9.5 The complete coordinate table

Design pixels within the strip `(0, 0, 1280, 78)`. Draw order is top to bottom of this table.
"tiny" = `_font(fonts, "tiny", 14)`; see §0.5.1 for the shim's argument-discarding quirk - with
a real FontBook these resolve to the **named ladder entry**, and the numeric argument is dead.

| # | Element | x | y | Anchor | Font | Colour | Condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Backdrop | 0 | 0 | top-left | - | `_hud_backdrop(theme)` (§7) | always |
| 2 | `"SCORE"` | 20 | 6 | left | tiny | `dim` | always |
| 3 | `"BEST {n:,}"` where `n = max(highscore, score)` | 250 | 6 | **right** | tiny | `shade(dim, 0.85)` | always |
| 4 | Score heat glow | 96 | 46 | centre | - | `UI_GOLD`, r 62, intensity `0.20 + 0.5·score_hit` | `score_hit > 0.01` |
| 5 | Odometer, value `int(score_disp + 0.5)` | 20 | 28 | top-left | `_font(fonts, "display", 34)` | `lerp(UI_WHITE, UI_GOLD, _step(0.35 + 0.5·score_hit, 5))` | always |
| 6 | `"LVL {level_index+1:02d}"` | 272 | 5 | left | tiny | `accent2` | always |
| 7 | `level_name.upper()` | 272 | 22 | left | `_font(fonts, "ui", 24, True)` | `theme.text` | always |
| 8 | Goal bar | rect `(272, 58, 250, 9)` | | | - | `goal_col` (below) | always |
| 9 | `"{min(eaten, goal)} / {goal}"` | 532 | 53 | left | tiny | `lerp(dim, UI_WHITE, _step(0.35 + 0.5·food_pop, 4))` | always |
| 10 | `"LIVES"` | 636 | 5 | left | tiny | `dim` | always |
| 11 | Life icons, `i = 0..icons-1` | `654 + i·pitch` | 44 | centre | - | §8, `alive = i < lives` | always |
| 12 | `"+{slots-6}"` | `658 + icons·pitch` | 37 | left | tiny | `dim` | `slots > 6` |
| 13 | Combo badge | 812 | 40 | centre | §8 | §8 | `combo > 1` |
| 14 | `"COMBO"` | 812 | 33 | **centre** | tiny | `shade(dim, 0.7)` | `combo <= 1` |
| 15 | `"BOOST"` | 900 | 7 | left | tiny | `label_col` (below) | always |
| 16 | Boost bar | rect `(958, 12, 222, 12)` | | | - | `boost_col` (below) | always |
| 17 | Boost glow | 1069 | 18 | centre | - | `boost_col`, r 90, intensity 0.35 | boosting **and** `frac > 0` |
| 18 | Effect chips, `i = 0..n-1` | `start + i·38` | 50 | centre | §8 | §8 | `n >= 1` |
| 19 | `"NO ACTIVE POWER-UPS"` | 1260 | 45 | **right** | tiny | `shade(dim, 0.6)` | no effects |
| 20 | Strip bloom | 640 | 76 | centre | - | `accent`, r 150, intensity 0.10 | always |

Row 3's `{:,}` is a **thousands separator**. `max(highscore, score)` means BEST tracks the live
score the moment it passes the stored best, rather than waiting for the run to end.

Row 20 is `_blit_glow(C.WINDOW_W * 0.5, C.HUD_H - 2, 150, accent, 0.10)` - it ties the strip to
the arena border below, and it is the main reason the clip in §9.1 exists.

### 9.6 Derived colours and geometry

**Goal bar** (ui.py:1065-1068):

```
goal_col = lerp(accent, UI_GOOD, _step(eaten / goal, 8))
if food_pop > 0.01:
    goal_col = lerp(goal_col, UI_WHITE, _step(food_pop * 0.8, 4))
draw_bar(rect, eaten / goal, goal_col)          # frac is NOT clamped here - draw_bar clamps
```

**Lives row** (ui.py:1074-1088):

```
shown_lives = max(lives, 0)
slots       = max(C.START_LIVES, shown_lives)    # 3 minimum, grows if lives were gained
max_icons   = 6
icons       = min(slots, max_icons)
span        = 140.0
pitch       = min(34.0, span / max(1, icons))    # 34 for <= 4 icons, then 28.0 at 5, 23.33 at 6
pop_i       = life_pop if i == shown_lives else 0.0
_draw_life_icon(654 + i * pitch, 44, theme, alive, t, phase = i * 1.7, pop = pop_i)
```

The pitch tightens rather than the row growing, because the icons must stay clear of the combo
badge at `x = 812`. The `pop` is applied to the **one slot at index `shown_lives`** - the slot
that just emptied (or just filled), not the whole row. Note the `+n` overflow label starts at
`658`, four pixels right of the icon origin `654`.

**Boost** (ui.py:1097-1109):

```
frac      = clamp(boost / boost_max, 0, 1)
low       = frac < (C.SNAKE_BOOST_MIN_TO_START / boost_max)      # 12 / 100 = 0.12
boost_col = UI_BAD if low else lerp(UI_WARN, accent2, _step(frac, 6))
label_col = lerp(dim, boost_col, _step(0.5 + 0.5 * pulse(t, 6.0), 4) if low else 0.4)
boosting  = bool(game.mouse_buttons.get(3)) and frac > 0.0
```

`low` is the "cannot start a boost" threshold, not "empty": below 12 % the bar turns `UI_BAD`
and the **label pulses** at 6 rad/s through `_step(..., 4)`, while above it the label sits at a
flat 0.4 blend. `mouse_buttons.get(3)` is the **right** mouse button - in the port that is
`game.pointer.boost` (integration.md §11 records the mapping, and it is a superset: touch boost
sets the same flag).

**Effect chips** (ui.py:1112-1125):

```
eff_list = [(str(k), float(v)) for k, v in list(effects)[:6]]    # hard cap 6, silently truncated
pitch    = 38
right    = C.WINDOW_W - 20                                        # 1260
start    = right - pitch * len(eff_list) + pitch * 0.5
x_i      = start + i * pitch
```

The block is right-aligned with **newest on the right**; `+ pitch * 0.5` converts the
right edge into the centre of the last chip. Ordering is whatever `effects` arrives in - §8.1
of integration.md says `effects.items()` is urgency-sorted, and that sort happens on the
*producing* side, in the effect stack, not here. The cap of 6 is silent: a seventh effect is
simply not drawn.

### 9.7 `_step` on colour blends - do **not** optimise this away

Six of the colour expressions above run their blend factor through `_step(x, n)` (§1.4), which
quantises to `n` levels. §1.4 explains that the *cache-key* quantisers become unnecessary in the
port because tint is free. **These are not those.** `_step` here is applied to a `lerp_color`
factor, so it produces visible banding in the ramp - the score colour moves through 5 discrete
steps as heat builds, the goal bar through 8 as it fills. That stepping is part of the look.
Keep `_step` on every one of: rows 5, 8, 9, 16 and the `label_col` / `goal_col` expressions.

### 9.8 The TS view

`web/src/ui/hud/Hud.ts` - one persistent view object, built once, mutated per frame:

```
Hud
  root: Container            (mask = the 1280x78 strip rect, built once)
  backdrop: Sprite           §7
  scoreCaption, bestLabel: Text
  scoreGlow: Sprite          visible only while score_hit > 0.01
  odometer: Odometer         §7
  lvlLabel, levelName: Text
  goalBar: Bar               §4
  goalLabel: Text
  livesLabel: Text
  lifeRow: LifeIcon[6]       §8, pooled - visibility and pitch set per frame
  overflowLabel: Text
  combo: ComboBadge          §8
  comboLabel: Text           the "COMBO" placeholder, swapped with the badge
  boostLabel: Text
  boostBar: Bar              §4
  boostGlow: Sprite
  chips: EffectChip[6]       §8, pooled
  noChipsLabel: Text
  stripBloom: Sprite
  anim: HudAnim              §7 - lives here, NOT on Game
```

`update(state, t)` does §9.2's dt derivation, then §9.4's bookkeeping, then writes positions,
tints and alphas. **A `Text`'s `.text` is assigned only when the string actually changes** -
seven of the nine labels here are static or change rarely, and rasterising glyphs every frame is
the one Python cost worth not copying. The four that do change (`BEST n`, `eaten / goal`,
`+n`, and the odometer's digits) should each keep their last string in a field and compare.

Pool sizes are fixed at 6 for both the life row and the chips, matching `max_icons` and the
`[:6]` truncation, so no allocation happens on a draw path.

### 9.9 What a test can cover

| Testable in vitest (pure) | Perceptual only (vs `captures/08-gameplay-ready.png`) |
|---|---|
| `score_disp` chase and the 0.6 snap | Backdrop gradient and the strip bloom |
| Every pop's rise and decay rate | Odometer digit roll |
| `pitch` for 1..6+ icons; the `+n` threshold | Life-icon and chip artwork |
| `low` threshold at exactly `frac = 0.12` | Colour banding from `_step` |
| Chip `start` / `x_i` for n = 1..6, and the silent truncation at 7 | Text metrics and their alignment |
| `max(highscore, score)` and the `{:,}` formatting | |
| Missing / null / unknown state keys not throwing | |
